# Architecture

This document maps what Nebulosa already has, where each feature belongs, and the contracts new features must follow. What is still to deliver is marked Planned, Partial, or Integration Required in the Capability Inventory; the next delivery is the Sequencer, whose composition boundary the Capture–Sequencer Compatibility Report fixes. Repository rules (boundaries, style, verification) live in [`AGENTS.md`](../AGENTS.md).

Reading order for a new contributor: the Glossary and the Architecture Map, then Adding a Feature, then the Workspace Responsibility Matrix and UI and Layout Contracts for anything visible, then the contract of each feature the change touches.

## Glossary

- **Connection**: an open INDI, Alpaca, or simulator client on the server (`api/src/connection.ts`), identified by its connection id. Saved connections (host, port, type) are workspace data the server keeps for every tab and browser (`api/src/workspace.ts`); open ones are server state shared by every tab.
- **Device**: one interface a connection reports (camera, mount, focuser, …), identified by connection id + device id (`DeviceSummary`). Several interfaces of one physical device share a hardware id.
- **Profile**: the workspace of an operator: its saved connection, equipment setup, optical presets, and preferences, kept by the server (`api/src/workspace.ts`) for every tab and browser and edited in the tab (`web/src/profile/`), which writes back what changed (`sync.ts`). Which profile is active is the tab's own.
- **Equipment setup** and **role**: the assignment, in a profile, of devices of its connection to operational roles (`EquipmentRole` in `web/src/equipment/roles.ts`: `MAIN_CAMERA`, `GUIDE_CAMERA`, `MOUNT`, `FOCUSER`, `FILTER_WHEEL`, `ROTATOR`, `GUIDE_OUTPUT`, `DOME`, `COVER`, `FLAT_PANEL`), edited in the Equipment Setup modal, which connects or disconnects the assigned devices one by one or all at once (Connect all, Disconnect all: `switchSetup`). Roles only choose devices in the UI; every command still names the exact device.
- **Operation**: a server-owned piece of device work holding devices under the `OperationRegistry` (`OperationKind` in `api/src/operations.ts`), observed through `operation:updated`.
- **Reservation**: a hold of the registry's `ResourceArbiter` on several devices, under which a caller inside the process composes tools (`ToolInvocation`, see Operation Lifecycle Rules); a manual command of a reserved device is refused as busy.
- **Session**: the record of a multi-step operation of one service (a capture, an Auto Focus run, a held mount nudge or dome rotation), with its own events.
- **Image document**: decoded pixels in the server's `ImageStore`, addressed by an image id; files, camera frames, and derived images are all documents.
- **Camera tab**: the permanent tab of Images for one connected camera, showing its frames and hosting its camera operations.
- **Lease**: a hold a client keeps on a server resource (a capture frame's document, a held motion) that the server releases when the client goes away.

## Architecture Map

| Layer   | Owns                                                                                                                                                           | Never owns                                        |
| ------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------- |
| `core/` | Algorithms and device protocols: INDI/Alpaca clients, commanders, orchestration, capture runner, solvers, star detection, ephemerides, eclipses                | Application state, HTTP, UI                       |
| `api/`  | Runtime services: connections, devices, operations, image documents, sky queries, the workspace, the operational database; HTTP routes and the event WebSocket | Presentation state                                |
| `web/`  | Presentation: workspaces, the active profile, selection, view state; mirrors of server state fed by HTTP and events                                            | Operations, device arbitration, scientific pixels |
| `app/`  | Wiring `api` and `web` into one `Bun.serve` and the build                                                                                                      | Business logic                                    |

Server state reaches every tab through `GET` snapshots plus the `/api/ws` event stream (`ServerEvent` in `api/src/websocket.ts`). A tab refreshes its snapshots on every socket open (`onOpen` in `web/src/main.tsx`), so a reload never loses running work. Each web store that mirrors server state is listed once in `SERVER_STORES` (`web/src/main.tsx`) with its event handler and its refresh. Events keep arriving while a snapshot is fetched, so an event may be applied before the response of an older snapshot: the stores of the operations, the captures, the sessions of the camera operations (Auto Focus, TPPA, DARV, Flat Wizard, Dark Library Wizard, Guiding, Target Centering), Live Stacking, the held mount nudges and dome rotations (`refreshPressSessions` of `web/src/press.ts`), and the mount pointing models and sessions record, through `SnapshotRefreshes` (`web/src/refresh.ts`), the keys their events change during a refresh, and the snapshot leaves those as the events set them (`merge`), so a session that ended during the request is never shown running again. The stores of the sessions of the camera operations share one mirror (`web/src/camera/sessions.ts`): the latest session of each camera ordered by its start and revision, the snapshot refresh, the complete record fetched apart when the store's `stale` rule says its summary changed, and the commands sent one at a time per camera; each store keeps only what its sessions hold and send. On the server, the services whose session runs under one operation (Auto Focus, TPPA, Target Centering, Flat Wizard, Dark Library Wizard, Guiding) keep their sessions in a `CameraSessionRegistry` (`api/src/camerasessions.ts`): the latest session of each camera, adopted in the same synchronous step as its operation starts, so its first published summary names its operation; the session each start token started, still known after a newer session of its camera replaced it, so a repeated token answers it; and the publication of a changed summary with its next revision, only while the session is still the latest of its camera. DARV, which runs an operation per measurement, and the mount pointing sessions, which outlive their pauses without an operation and run one per stretch of work, keep their own.

One stream is the exception: the INDI property stream of a physical device (`api/src/indi.ts`, see INDI Properties Contract) is never broadcast. A tab asks for it with an `indi:subscribe` message over its own `/api/ws` socket and ends it with `indi:unsubscribe` (`ClientMessage`, the only messages the server reads from a socket); it receives a full snapshot on that socket, then the changes, so snapshot and changes form one ordered stream without a `GET`; the subscription belongs to the socket, not to the tab's client id, and ends with it.

The control states of the devices of the active profile's connection are mirrored once in the web, by `controlRuntime` (`web/src/equipment-control/control.ts`), from `GET /api/connections/:id/states` and the `<type>:updated` events. A view that follows one device, such as the Sky mount (`mountView`), Mount Nudge, the camera capabilities of Camera Acquisition and Auto Focus, or the sensor geometry of the camera roles that Sky Framing and the Calculator use (`CameraCapabilities.sensor`, `liveSensors` of `web/src/sky/sensors.ts`), reads that mirror (`watchedDeviceState`) and never fetches or tracks the device's state apart.

Each `api` service exposes its routes through a `create<Name>Routes(service)` factory in its own module; `api/src/index.ts` only composes the services, spreads their routes, and serves `/api/health` and `/api/ws`. One route is composed by `app/src/main.ts` instead: `createAboutRoutes` (`api/src/about.ts`) serves the identity of the distribution, which `app` owns (its package version, and the commits and compile time `app/build.ts` inlines into the executable), so `api` never imports `app`.

Every error response of `api` is `{ error, code }` (`ApiError` and `errorResponse` in `api/src/http.ts`): a message meant for people and a stable code a client branches on, never the message. Each service names its codes in a `<Name>ErrorCode` union (`CommandErrorCode` of `api/src/commands.ts` for the device commands, `StarDetectionErrorCode`, `PlateSolveErrorCode`, `CaptureFrameErrorCode`, …), and a route may add documented fields, such as the current state after a `conflict`.

Events of `/api/ws` (`ServerEvent`), by `type`: `connection:open`, `connection:close` (its `cause` is `requested` for a disconnect asked by a tab or the disposal of the registry, `lost` when the client itself reported the close), `device:added`, `device:updated`, `device:removed`, `<type>:updated` for the control state of each managed device type but the camera, whose state is `camera:capabilities`, `operation:updated`, `capture:updated`, `capture:frame`, `captureFrame:updated`, `captureSource:updated`, `autofocus:updated`, `tppa:updated`, `mountPointing:updated`, `mountPointing:activation`, `mountPointing:modelUpdated`, `mountPointing:modelRemoved`, `targetCentering:updated`, `darv:updated`, `flatWizard:updated`, `darkLibraryWizard:updated`, `calibrationLibrary:changed`, `calibrationLibrary:jobUpdated`, `calibrationLibrary:masterUpdated`, `imageStacking:changed`, `imageStacking:jobUpdated`, `liveStacking:updated`, `liveStacking:closed`, `liveStacking:products`, `guiding:updated`, `guiding:event`, `guiding:telemetry`, `guiding:frame`, `mount:nudge`, `mountRemoteControl:updated`, `meridianFlip:updated`, `meridianFlip:transition`, `dome:motion`, `focuserBacklash:updated`, `externalTool:updated`, `savedConnection:updated`, `savedConnection:removed`, `observingSite:updated`, `observingSite:removed`, `profile:updated`, `profile:removed`, `stars:updated`, `streaks:updated`, `plateSolving:updated`, `annotations:updated`, `imageProcessing:updated`, `notification:created`, `satellites:changed`, and `indi:deviceAdded`, `indi:deviceUpdated`, `indi:deviceRemoved` for the INDI device directory. Sent only to the socket that subscribed: `indi:snapshot`, `indi:propertyDefined`, `indi:propertyUpdated`, `indi:propertyRemoved`, `indi:message`, and `indi:subscriptionError`. Events never carry pixels or secrets (a frame is named by its image id); a client fetches pixels and full records through the route of their owner.

The events of the connections and devices, the operations, the pointing models and their activations, the Meridian Flip Monitors, and the sessions of Camera Acquisition, the camera operations, Calibration Library, Image Stacking, and Live Stacking are published through one `ServerEventDispatcher` (`api/src/events.ts`, `publish` of `api/src/index.ts`); the others (the workspace, the notifications, the held motions, Mount Remote Control, the focuser backlash, the external tools, the analysis jobs, the capture frames, the satellites, the INDI directory) go to `broadcast` directly, so neither history nor an in-process observer sees them. The dispatcher's subscribers are, in order, the WebSocket broadcast, the operation history, and the notification history, so a tab sees a change before the notification about it, and a service of the process that observes the others (a caller composing tools, the Meridian Flip Monitors) subscribes after them and unsubscribes with the function `subscribe` returned. Delivery is synchronous, in memory only, and never replayed, so an event is an observation, never the outcome of a command; an observer that throws is reported and skipped, never reaching the next observers nor the device operation that published the event. Events another feature will need (a step of a sequencer run) are added to `ServerEvent` by the service that owns them, when it exists, and published through the same dispatcher, as the Meridian Flip Monitor publishes `meridianFlip:transition`.

## Adding a Feature

The path every new capability follows, so it lands in one place and is observable from every tab:

1. **Toolkit first.** If the algorithm or protocol is missing, it belongs to `nebulosa.ts` (`core/`), not to this repository.
2. **Server service.** A class in its own `api/src/<name>.ts` taking its collaborators in the constructor (devices, client lookup, `OperationRegistry`, `ImageStore`, `ExternalToolsStore`, and `publish` for the events of its operations and sessions or `broadcast` for the others, see Routes and Events), with a `create<Name>Routes(service)` factory beside it, composed in `api/src/index.ts`. Device work runs as an operation; image analyses use the shared `AnalysisScheduler`, unless the analysis is one short request a person is waiting on (Star Profile Inspector Contract); settings that belong to the server instance, and any other state that must survive a restart, are rows of a table of `api/src/database/migrations/0001_initial.sql`, read and written through the `AppDatabase` the store receives (see Central Database), never a file or a database of their own. A capability a caller inside the process may compose answers through a typed `admit…` method (`admit`, `admitGoTo`, `admitMove`) taking an optional `ToolInvocation`, returning its identity and end or a `CommandRefusal`, and runs every operation of it under `operations.scope(invocation)` (see Operation Lifecycle Rules); its route only maps that to a `Response`.
3. **Contract.** Payload, state, and event types exported from `api/src/index.ts`, which the web imports with `import type { … } from 'api'`; error codes named; the event added to `ServerEvent`.
4. **Web mirror.** A `valtio` store fed by a `GET` snapshot and the event, registered once in `SERVER_STORES` (`web/src/main.tsx`), with pure, injectable-`fetch` tests.
5. **One place in the UI.** An entry in the registry of its workspace (`IMAGE_TOOL_DEFINITIONS` of `web/src/images/tools.ts`, `TOOL_DEFINITIONS` of `web/src/tools/tools.ts`, `SkyPanelView` of `web/src/sky/sky.ts`, `CONTROLS` of `web/src/equipment-control/DeviceDetail.tsx`, or a new `WORKSPACE_IDS` entry of `web/src/shell/route.ts`), following UI and Layout Contracts. A registry entry may exist before its view only as planned.
6. **Preferences.** Per-operator form values go into the profile (`ProfileSettings` of `web/src/profile/schema.ts`): a preference the server keeps is a key of `PREFERENCES` in `web/src/profile/sync.ts`, of `ProfilePreferenceKey` in `api/src/workspace.ts`, and of the `CHECK` of `profile_preferences`, and a camera form a key of `CameraSettings` (`schema.ts`), of `CAMERA_FORMS` (`sync.ts`), of `ProfileCameraForm`, and of the `CHECK` of `profile_camera_settings`; a field the server never keeps is view state: it says so in `schema.ts` and is a key of `VIEW_FIELDS` and `VIEW_STORAGE` in `web/src/profile/view.ts`. A change to the shape of a kept value raises `PROFILE_SETTINGS_VERSION` (an added optional field does not), never a migration; per-device forms are keyed by the device (connection id and device id), never by a role.
7. **Credits.** An external service, dataset, published model or method, or program a feature starts using gets its entry in `CREDIT_GROUPS` (`web/src/about/about.ts`), with the license or acknowledgment its publisher asks for.
8. **This document.** The inventory row, the matrix rows, the routes and events of Routes and Events, and the feature's contract section are updated in the same commit.

## Capability Inventory

Status: **Implemented** (works end to end), **Integration Required** (exists in `core`, not exposed by `api`/`web`), **Partial**, **Planned** (nothing yet).

| Capability                                                                                 | Status               | Where                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | -------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Shell, routes, workspaces, status bar                                                      | Implemented          | `web/src/shell/` (`route.ts`, `workspaces.ts`, `WorkspaceLayout.tsx`, `statusbar/`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| About page (distribution identity)                                                         | Implemented          | `web/src/about/`, route `/about` opened from the bottom of the navigation rail, over `GET /api/about` (`api/src/about.ts`); commits and compile time inlined by `app/build.ts`, absent when running from source                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| Profiles (versioned)                                                                       | Implemented          | `web/src/profile/` (`schema.ts` with `PROFILE_SETTINGS_VERSION`, store and actions in `profile.ts`, synchronization with the server in `sync.ts`), kept by `api/src/workspace.ts`; the active profile and the view fields the server never keeps are the tab's, in browser storage (`view.ts`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| Connections (INDI, Alpaca, simulator)                                                      | Implemented          | `api/src/connection.ts`, `web/src/connection/`                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| Equipment Setup (roles, telescope and guide telescope optics)                              | Implemented          | `web/src/equipment/` (`roles.ts`, `equipment.ts`, `EquipmentModal.tsx`, `availability.ts`), opened from Management                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |

## Workspace Responsibility Matrix

Each feature has exactly one place in the UI. Workspaces share services, not interfaces.

| Workspace                           | Responsibility                                                  | Features                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | Registry                                                                                                    |
| ----------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Images                              | Documents, image work, and the camera operations of camera tabs | Image Tools: Image Inspector, Plate Solving, Star Detection, Star Profile Inspector, Streak Detection, Annotation, Image Processing. Camera Operations: Camera Acquisition (labeled Acquisition), Live Stacking, Auto Focus, Mount Nudge, Guiding, DARV, TPPA, Pointing Model Builder, Target Centering, Flat Wizard, Dark Library Wizard | `IMAGE_TOOL_DEFINITIONS` (`web/src/images/tools.ts`)                                                        |
| Sky                                 | Exploration, planning, contextual mount control                 | Planetarium, search, time bar (toggled from the toolbar, view state of the tab), mount center and follow; panel views Object Inspector (with slew; the Sun, the Moon, artificial satellites, and the mount marker have their own views: the Sun, Moon, Satellite, and Mount Inspectors), Observer, Display (layers), Framing (FOV)        | `SkyPanelView` (`web/src/sky/sky.ts`), panels in `SkyWorkspace.tsx`                                         |
| Tools                               | Standalone utilities, no camera or document needed              | Calculator, Astronomical Time Converter, Solar Eclipse Map, Lunar Eclipse Map, Close Approaches, Astro Weather, Artificial Satellites, Calibration Library, Image Stacking, Mount Remote Control, Mount Pointing Model; Alpaca Server planned                                                                                             | `TOOL_DEFINITIONS` (`web/src/tools/tools.ts`), `/tools/<id>`                                                |
| Sequencer                           | Configuring and observing server-run automation                 | Planned                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | a future entry in `WORKSPACE_IDS`                                                                           |
| Equipment Control (Management page) | Manual control of every device of the profile's connection      | Camera (identity and cooling), mount, guide output, focuser, filter wheel, rotator, cover, flat panel, dew heater, dome; INDI properties of every device the INDI server reports, managed or not                                                                                                                                          | `CONTROLS` (`web/src/equipment-control/DeviceDetail.tsx`), `/equipment[/<device id>[/indi]]`                |
| Settings (top bar gear button)      | Settings of the server instance and of the active profile       | Sections chosen by a header selector, like the Images tool selector: External Tools (server), Observing Sites (server), Date & Time (profile), Status Bar (profile)                                                                                                                                                                       | `SETTINGS_SECTION_IDS`, `/settings/<section>`, `/settings/external-tools[/<tool id>]` (`web/src/settings/`) |
| About (rail footer button)          | Identity of the running distribution                            | Logo, version, build commits and time, server runtime and platform, Copy Version Info; author, license, repositories, and the credits of the external services, data, published models and methods, and programs in use (`CREDIT_GROUPS` of `web/src/about/about.ts`), shown even when the server cannot be reached                       | `/about` (`web/src/about/`)                                                                                 |

Connections, Equipment Setup, and profiles are modals of the shell (`ConnectionModal`, `EquipmentModal`, `ProfileModal`), not routes. The URL path is the source of truth for navigation (`web/src/shell/route.ts`): `/images`, `/sky`, `/tools[/<tool>]` (`/tools/solar-eclipse-map/<eclipse id>` and `/tools/lunar-eclipse-map/<eclipse id>` for one eclipse, `/tools/calibration-library/<group id>` for one group, `/tools/image-stacking/<project id>` for one project, `/tools/artificial-satellites/<NORAD id>` for one satellite; an unknown tool opens the catalog), `/equipment[/<device>[/indi]]`, `/settings/<section>` (`/settings/external-tools[/<tool>]`, `/settings/observing-sites[/<site>]`, `/settings/date-time`; an unknown section opens the first), `/about`; any other path opens Images, and the URL is rewritten to the canonical path of the route (`watchHistory`).

An image tool declares its `outputs` (`ImageOperationOutput`): `display` (a new rendering of the document), `derived` (a new temporary document), or `analysis` (data and overlays that leave the pixels untouched); the first is its default, and it names the panel's main action (Apply to View, Run Pipeline, Run Analysis). Camera operations have no outputs: their frames reach the tab as the camera's latest frame.

Rules:

- A registry entry without an implementation is **planned**: listed with its reason, never selectable or openable. Placeholder panels are not created.
- Selecting a mode, tool, tab, or profile never starts, stops, or redirects an operation.
- There is no Capture workspace: acquisition and the assisted operations of equipment are camera operations of the Images camera tabs; `/capture` is an unknown path.
- The bottom area of a camera tab (`CameraBottom` of `web/src/images/ImagesWorkspace.tsx`) shows the capture frames carousel and, once its camera has the session, a switch to the Focus Curve of Auto Focus (`FocusCurve`), the Guiding Graph, or the Pointing Map of a mount pointing session (`PointingMapView`): a choice per tab, in memory, that never changes the frame selected. Each view belongs to its session and keeps updating whatever frame or tool is shown; one whose session is gone shows the carousel.
- Auto Focus is a camera operation of a camera tab too (`web/src/images/AutoFocusTool.tsx`, over `AutoFocusSections`). It focuses the tab's camera with the focuser its context associates (`NO_FOCUSER` explains the Main Camera rule and offers Equipment Setup otherwise; `autoFocusBlocker` judges a start), with the exposure time of its own form (`ExposureTimeInput`, shared with Camera Acquisition and the other camera operations that take one) and the camera settings of Camera Acquisition, shown under the exposure time in the Acquisition section (`CameraAcquisitionSection`), those of the session while it runs. The form's "Return to the start position when stopped" (on by default) sets `restoreOnStop`, and the session state shows the progress of the frame being exposed (`FrameProgress`). Its frames are shown by the tab's Latest, with the markers of the tab's own star overlay; no viewer of its own.
- Mount Nudge is a camera operation of a camera tab too (`web/src/images/MountNudgeTool.tsx`, over `MountNudgeSections`): it moves the mount the tab's context associates (the setup's `MOUNT`), named with the camera in its Equipment section, and offers Equipment Setup when none is assigned. Directions are the mount's axes, never the image's. A press is released on pointer up, cancel, or lost capture, window blur, a hidden page, a change of the mount followed (a profile switch, `watchNudgeMount`), and the panel's unmount; the contextual panel is keyed by the tab, so switching tool or camera tab unmounts it. The toolbar of a camera tab toggles the central reticle over its frames (`viewer/Reticle`), a presentation choice per tab kept in memory; nudging needs no new exposure. Equipment Control opens it for the setup's mount on the Main Camera's tab, or the first camera tab of the connection (`connectionCameraTab`), and is disabled when the connection has no camera tab.
- TPPA is a camera operation of a camera tab too (`web/src/images/TppaTool.tsx`, over `TppaSections` of `web/src/camera/TppaPanel.tsx`, store `web/src/camera/tppa.ts`). It aligns with the tab's camera and the setup's `MOUNT`; a mount the TPPA can never use (alt-az, no west-east motion or abort, no slew-rate preset: `tppaMountSupport`) is `unsupported` and offers Equipment Setup, and `tppaBlocker` judges a start (mount state known, a preset the mount announces, a site, unparked, tracking or able to switch tracking, the plate solver configured). The form (direction, slew rate, rotation, settle and refinement delays, plate solver, and the "Stop the tracking when the session ends" checkbox, on by default, `stopTracking`) is the camera's and shows the values applied while a session runs, its exposure time included; the camera settings are the camera's shared block, shown under the exposure time in the Acquisition section (`CameraAcquisitionSection`). The session state shows the progress of the frame being exposed (`FrameProgress`; a refinement frame once the driver reports it) and whether the tracking the session started was stopped or left on. Its frames are shown by the tab's Latest with the correction guide (`web/src/camera/TppaOverlay.tsx`) over the frame the latest estimate was solved on; the Image Inspector names the step of a TPPA frame (pose, rejected candidate, refinement).
- DARV is a camera operation of a camera tab too (`web/src/images/DarvTool.tsx`, over `DarvSections` of `web/src/camera/DarvPanel.tsx`, store `web/src/camera/darv.ts`). It measures with the tab's camera and the setup's `MOUNT`; a mount DARV can never use (alt-az, no west-east motion or abort, no slew-rate preset: `darvMountSupport`) is `unsupported` and offers Equipment Setup, and `darvBlocker` judges a start (mount state known, a preset the mount announces, unparked, tracking or able to switch tracking; no site or plate solver is needed). The form (axis, direction of the first motion, slew rate, leg duration) is the camera's and shows the values applied while a measurement runs; the exposure is derived from the leg duration (`darvExposureTime`) and only shown, and the camera settings come from the shared block, whose exposure time is never sent nor changed. While a measurement runs the panel shows its step among the phases and a bar of the exposure elapsed, from the `exposureStartedAt` the server records (`darvProgress`); the analysis, of unknown length, pulses. The panel repeats the measurement in the open session while its mount is the setup's, compares the drift only with the previous measurement of equal settings, and finishes the session. Its frames are shown by the tab's Latest with the paired trails (`web/src/camera/DarvOverlay.tsx`) over the frame they were found on; the Image Inspector names the measurement and its result.
- Flat Wizard is a camera operation of a camera tab too (`web/src/images/FlatWizardTool.tsx`, over `FlatWizardSections` of `web/src/camera/FlatWizardPanel.tsx`, store `web/src/camera/flatwizard.ts`). It acquires with the tab's camera and, when the camera is the Main Camera of the setup, the setup's `FILTER_WHEEL`, `FLAT_PANEL`, and `COVER` (`CameraOperationContext.wheel`, `flatPanel`, and `cover`); an unassigned wheel or panel makes that step manual and an unassigned cover is left as it is, while an assigned one that cannot be used blocks the start (`flatWizardBlocker`) instead of falling back to a manual step, and any other camera uses neither. The form (filter groups with filter slot, frame count, exposure bounds, DN target, and panel brightness; directory, effective clipping level, proposed at `DEFAULT_FLAT_WIZARD_EFFECTIVE_CLIP` because a sensor can top out under the 65535 DN of the file, settle time, trial and rejection limits) is the camera's and shows the values applied while a session runs; the camera settings come from the shared block, whose exposure time is never sent nor changed (`flatWizardRequest`). The panel shows the phase, the progress of the frame being exposed (`FrameProgress` of `web/src/camera/FrameProgress.tsx`, shared by the camera operations that expose frames: a bar with the time elapsed and left, from the driver's countdown, pulsing with its times shown as absent until it reports one), the filter in progress, the exposure being tried or chosen, the levels in DN of the latest frame, and, while the session waits for the operator, the instruction and the Continue button that confirms that step's id; nothing else confirms a step. Stop cancels the session. Results list each group's saved and discarded frames, exposure, stability, failure, and a light left on. Its frames are shown by the tab's Latest, and the Image Inspector names the origin Flat Wizard.
- Guiding is a camera operation of a camera tab too (`web/src/images/GuidingTool.tsx`, over `GuidingSections` of `web/src/camera/GuidingPanel.tsx`, store `web/src/camera/guiding.ts`). It guides with the tab's camera and the setup's `GUIDE_OUTPUT`, which only the `GUIDE_CAMERA` has (`CameraOperationContext.guideOutput`; any other camera is told so and offered Equipment Setup), snooping the setup's devices like a capture and detecting the stars with the detector of the profile's Star Detection and its settings, as Auto Focus does (not its acceptance criteria: the tracker filters the stars, from 5 sigma by default); a detector set to ASTAP while ASTAP is not configured blocks the start (`guidingBlocker`). A busy guide output does not block looping. The panel edits the camera's guiding form, shows the camera settings of Camera Acquisition its guide frames take under the guide exposure in the Acquisition section (`CameraAcquisitionSection`, the profile's, since a session keeps no copy to show), shows the progress of the guide exposure as the other camera operations do (`FrameProgress`), the Star Profile of the guide star (`web/src/camera/GuidingStarProfile.tsx`: both cuts with the half maximum, and every measurement of the profile with widths in arcseconds when the pixel scale is known), the session (state, lock, calibration rates and angles, RMS in pixels and arcseconds when the guide focal length is known, the outcome of the latest command, the failure), and offers the commands of the current state. The guide focal length is the effective focal length (focal length times reducer or Barlow) of the profile's Guide Telescope in Equipment Setup (`ProfileSettings.guideTelescope`, `guideFocalLength`), sent as the session optics when set; the panel shows it, or offers Equipment Setup while it is not set. Calculate sets the calibration step from the guide focal length, the camera's pixel size and binning, the output's RA guide rate, and the mount's declination, for 25 px of travel in 12 RA steps as the PHD2 calculator (`guidingCalibrationPulse`); while it cannot, the panel names each missing input and where it comes from, or says the RA motion vanishes (`guidingCalibrationBlocker`). The Session section shows the step and a bar per leg of a running calibration (`web/src/camera/GuidingCalibrationProgress.tsx`). The viewer draws the guider overlay (`GuidingOverlay` of `web/src/camera/GuidingOverlay.tsx`: accepted and rejected stars, circled with a radius of at least 4 screen pixels and at least their HFD so the circle surrounds the star at any zoom, search square, lock cross, measurement) only over the session's newest previewed frame, and while the Guiding tool is selected and the guide output is not held, a click on that frame selects the star nearest it (`/target`, in 0-based pixel centres). The Guiding Graph of the tab's bottom area (`web/src/camera/GuidingGraph.tsx`) draws the RA and DEC errors of the latest `GUIDING_GRAPH_SAMPLES` (100) guide steps (arcseconds when the pixel scale is known), the corrections as bars, gaps for lost frames, and markers for dithers, pauses, resumes, guiding starts, and lost locks.
- Dark Library Wizard is a camera operation of a camera tab too (`web/src/images/DarkLibraryWizardTool.tsx`, over `DarkLibraryWizardSections` of `web/src/camera/DarkLibraryWizardPanel.tsx`, store `web/src/camera/darklibrarywizard.ts`). It uses the tab's camera and, when the camera is the Main Camera of the setup, the setup's `FILTER_WHEEL`, `FLAT_PANEL`, and `COVER`; an option whose device is not assigned cannot be selected, a selected device that cannot be used blocks the start (`darkLibraryWizardBlocker`) instead of falling back to a manual step, and a device nothing selected never blocks it. Its sections are Equipment, Capture Plan, Light Protection, Thermal Control, Output & Quality, Session, and Controls. The Capture Plan starts with its Frame Type, Dark or Dark-Flat (the `purpose` of the plan), which Prepare Matching Dark Flats of the Flat Wizard sets too. The rows of the plan are the form: a group is added, moved, removed, or duplicated right below itself (`duplicateDarkGroup`), and a copy blocks the start until one of its settings changes, since a repeated group is refused. Each exposure is typed in the unit of `ExposureTimeInput`, and the estimates of frames, exposure, and disk space are labelled as estimates. Gain, offset, binning, subframe, and readout mode come from Camera Acquisition (`darkLibraryWizardCapture`, fitted as a capture is), and only the exposure, target temperature, and frame count are the group's; nothing in Camera Acquisition is changed. The disk space follows those fitted settings: the subframe or the sensor, each side divided by its binning and rounded up, 16 bits per sample, three channels for a readout mode named RGB, and the padded FITS header and data. Start asks for no confirmation: above it, a warning (`darkLibraryWizardWarning`) names what no device verifies, the manual cap, the opacity of the filter at the dark slot, and an unlit panel when it is the only barrier, and starting is the operator's assertion of it. Every start makes a new plan; plans are never listed, completed, or reused in the tab.
- Live Stacking is a camera operation of a camera tab too (`web/src/images/LiveStackingTool.tsx`, over `LiveStackingSections` of `web/src/camera/LiveStackingPanel.tsx`, store `web/src/camera/livestacking.ts`). It never takes a frame: it integrates the frames Camera Acquisition takes with the tab's camera, whichever tab or output mode, so it needs no equipment and holds no device, and its panel only links to Acquisition. Its sections are the session (Start, Pause, Resume, Stop, Abort, New Field, Reset after a confirmation, Close, the frame counts, the capture it follows, and the memory), the masters chosen per exact filter name, each filter a collapsible section that can be removed (filters chosen among the slot names of the connected filter wheel of the Main Camera, or None, the filter of frames without one, when there is no connected wheel; masters from the Calibration Library lists, never chosen for the operator, and only flats without a filter offered for None) with what to do with other filters, the recipe (locked while a session runs), what the tab shows (Latest frame, one stack chosen among the stacks of every field, or a color composition of the mono stacks of one field by the presets of Image Stacking or a custom mix of weights and gains, saved as a linear color FITS). A stack shown is saved from the Save control of the viewer toolbar, and the Image Inspector describes the stacks of the snapshot shown (filter and field, frames and total exposure, acquisition settings, masters, sensor temperatures). The recipe and masters are the `liveStacking` form of the camera in the profile.
- Camera Acquisition is the camera operation of a camera tab (`web/src/images/CameraAcquisitionTool.tsx`): the acquisition panel of the tab's camera (`CameraAcquisitionSections`), its availability judged on the camera itself (`deviceAvailability`). Its Dithering section (`web/src/camera/DitheringSection.tsx`) turns the dithering of the fixed and loop LIGHT captures of the Main Camera on and sets its cadence and method: Guided, through the guiding session of the setup's GUIDE_CAMERA with the amount, RA only, and settle of that camera's Guiding form, shown read-only; or Mount, moving the setup's MOUNT by guide pulses through the setup's GUIDE_OUTPUT or a small GoTo, with its amount in arcseconds, pattern, RA only, and mechanical settle, and under Advanced the guide pulse directions and limit of the mount. Start is refused with the reason while the chosen method cannot dither (no guiding session guiding, no mount or guide output assigned); the capture request carries the frozen dither (`captureDither` of `web/src/camera/capture.ts`), and a running capture shows the dither of its request, never the form. Another camera never dithers. The status shows Dithering or Settling and the dithers done, and the status bar shows Dithering on the main camera chip. The toolbar of a camera tab shows the state of its camera's capture (phase, time left, frame count, file state of the latest frame, or why it failed) whatever tool is selected; switching tool, frame, or tab never stops a capture.
- The devices a camera operation uses are named as links to their page in Equipment Control (`DeviceLink` of `web/src/equipment-control/DeviceLink.tsx`, route `/equipment/<device id>`): the camera in the camera settings of Camera Acquisition (`CameraSettings` of `web/src/camera/ExposureSettings.tsx`), and every device of the Equipment section of the other camera operations; Mount Remote Control, Mount Pointing Model, and the status bar details link their devices the same way.
- An Images tool is an image tool or a camera operation (`category` of `IMAGE_TOOL_DEFINITIONS`), listed under its heading in one selector (`imageToolsFor`). Camera operations are listed only on a camera tab: the selector of a file or derived document shows only the image tools. Its equipment is the `CameraOperationContext` of the tab (`web/src/images/cameracontext.ts`): the physical camera of the tab; the `FOCUSER` of the active setup only when the camera is its `MAIN_CAMERA` (the setup has flat roles and no optical train, so no other camera has a focuser); the `MOUNT` of the active setup for every camera of the watched connection; the `GUIDE_OUTPUT` of the active setup only when the camera is its `GUIDE_CAMERA` (`CameraOperationContext.guideOutput`), so no other camera guides; the `FILTER_WHEEL`, `FLAT_PANEL`, and `COVER` only for the `MAIN_CAMERA` (`wheel`, `flatPanel`, `cover`); and the setup's snooped devices for the Main Camera, the mount alone for the Guide Camera, and none for a camera without a role. A missing assignment is reported by the operation's panel, never replaced by another connected device.
- The display settings of a document are changed from the viewer toolbar, whatever tool is selected (`web/src/images/DisplayToolbar.tsx`, over `Popover` of `web/src/ui/Popover.tsx`); the Image Inspector only shows the parameters of the current rendering. Debayer, Stretch, Brightness & Contrast, and Color each open a popup, one at a time, and Invert is a toggle. Debayer (enabled and pattern) and Invert apply as soon as they change; Stretch, Brightness & Contrast, and Color edit a draft started from the applied settings each time their popup opens, applied by their Apply to View and discarded when the popup closes, and their Reset Parameters restores the neutral values (`ORIGINAL_DISPLAY_SETTINGS`) in the draft without applying them. Each apply sends only its own settings as a patch (`applyDisplay` in `web/src/images/images.ts`), merged into the settings being applied or the applied ones, so a toggle during a rendering builds on it. An auto stretch is the toolkit's `autoStf` with the preset chosen in the Stretch popup (Low, Medium, High, Moon, or Planet; Medium is the default, the plain adaptive display function), linked or per channel, memoized per document by base image, linking, and preset. Presets always sample the whole image, without a region or mask, so Moon and Planet assume a target filling most of the frame. Debayer is disabled for an image that is not a CFA mosaic (mono with a BAYERPAT, `isMosaic`), Color for a grayscale rendering, and every control until the document is first rendered. The settings applied are saved in the profile (see Persistence and State): a new file or derived document starts with the global ones and a camera tab with those of its camera (`initialDisplaySettings`, debayering turned off where it does not apply or the image's own pattern is not trusted), or with the image's defaults when none were saved. Images derived by a pipeline get the global settings as they are, even when a step already stretched them.
- Saving and downloading are a control of the viewer toolbar, for every document (file, derived, or camera tab), whatever tool is selected (`web/src/images/SaveImagePopover.tsx`); no panel saves or downloads. Its popup chooses the format: FITS or XISF, the pixels the server holds in memory encoded in the scale of their source with the header (`ImageStore.encode`), or JPEG, the rendering the viewer shows (the original one during Show Original, the applied one otherwise: `shownDisplaySettings`), encoded by the worker exactly as a rendering with those settings but outside the render queue, so no rendering supersedes it. Save to Server opens the file explorer in save mode listing the files of the format (`SaveImageModal.tsx`; the format's extension is added unless the name already has one of it, `saveFileName`), starting in the folder the profile saved to last (`ProfileSettings.lastSaveDirectory`, changed only by a write the server confirmed and apart from `lastImageDirectory`, where Open Image starts), then the folder of the document, then `lastImageDirectory`, and posts `/api/images/:id/save` (`settings` for JPEG, 422 when they cannot be applied); an existing file is replaced only after a confirmation, and never the file of an open document. Download has the browser save `/api/images/:id/file` (FITS or XISF, named after the document with the format's extension instead of its own) or the object URL of the JPEG shown. Both write a copy: the tab keeps its name and source, and only a derived document whose pixels (FITS or XISF) were written to a server file stops being unsaved and shows that file's folder; a JPEG never saves it.

## UI and Layout Contracts

The rules a new screen or a layout proposal follows, so it fits the existing shell without new layout systems.

Shell (`web/src/shell/ApplicationShell.tsx`), always mounted while navigating:

- **Top bar** (`web/src/layout/TopBar.tsx`, `h-14`, 49 px): logo, the Management menu (Connections, Equipment Setup, Equipment Control), then the observing site menu (`ObservingSiteMenu`: a pin with the active profile's site, or Set Location, listing every saved site to select in one click, No Active Site, and Manage Sites; the name hidden below `sm`; choosing changes the profile only, never a device), the connection indicator, the notification bell (the unread count of the active profile's connection and the notification panel; see Notifications Contract), the gear button of the Settings page (highlighted while it is shown), and the profile menu.
- **Navigation rail** (`NavigationRail.tsx`, `w-16`, 56 px): one button per entry of `WORKSPACES`, in a region that scrolls when the window is short, and the About page button anchored below it, never scrolled out of view; pages opened from Management or the top bar are not listed.
- **Workspace or page**: the component of the route; only it is swapped on navigation.
- **Status bar** (`StatusBar.tsx`, `h-7`, 24.5 px, selectors and items in `web/src/shell/statusbar/`): everything the active profile has, in as little room as it can. The tab's link to the application server is a server icon, green while the event socket is open (`notificationsState.server`) and red otherwise, with the link and the selected connection in its title (the top bar shows and manages the connection). Then a chip per assigned role whose device is present and reported connected (none for an unassigned or disconnected device, or a role the profile hides), in the order main camera, filter wheel, focuser, rotator, mount, guide camera, dome, cover, flat panel, each an icon and the readings the profile asks of it (`STATUS_CHIP_FIELDS`, by default its status and main measurement), its name and full reading in its title, opening its exact device in Equipment Control (`roleChip`). The status says what the device is doing: the camera's capture phase, or Exposing for an exposure of any other tool (`exposing` of its state); the mount's motion (Parking, Homing, Slewing, Moving), or parked, tracking, or idle; Moving for a focuser, rotator, cover, or dome, whose status also says when it parks or homes; Changing for a wheel; the guide camera's running session (dither, settle, the RMS of the latest guide steps in arcseconds only when the session knows the pixel scale and in guide pixels otherwise, never an earlier RMS outside guiding, or the guider state); the cover open or closed; the flat panel's light on or off. The measurements are the sensor temperature in °C only when the driver reports a finite one and the cooler power of a camera, the mount's equatorial coordinates of date, the wheel's filter name at its 0-based slot, or the 1-based slot number, the focuser's position in steps and temperature, the rotator's mechanical angle in degrees (not a sky position angle), the dome's shutter and azimuth, and the flat panel's brightness. The right group holds every running operation that holds a device of the active profile's server connection (never another connection's, and `guideOutput.guiding` only through the `camera.guiding` of its session, by exact operation id), each as its icon, name, and progress followed by its Abort when it can be interrupted (`barOperations`): a capture shows the frames taken of those requested and the completed percentage, counting the frame in flight by its exposure (`capturePercent`), or the frames taken and the percentage of the current exposure for a loop, or Stopping, and any other operation the steps it reports. Then come the count of failures of that connection's devices (connect and driver failures of `deviceState.errors` and refused commands of `controlRuntime.errors`, each only while its store tracks that connection, listed in its title) and the details button. Before the failures, the Meridian Flip Monitor of the profile's mount shows while it is enabled (`MeridianFlipItem`, `data-fixed`): its icon and status (`meridianFlipStatusText`: the headline of `meridianFlipHeadline`, with `T−m:ss` to or `T+m:ss` since the transit while it monitors a current forecast, interpolated from the server's sample), in the tone of the headline (a warning or a danger only for a flip candidate in or past the window), opening the tool; it never shows for another mount, and it commands nothing. The operations are fitted first, measured on hidden copies of them before the browser paints and again whenever the bar or an item changes size (`fitOperations`): those that can be interrupted first, in start order, skipping one that does not fit, the first one always shown. The chips are fitted in the room left (`fitItems`): moving devices first, then running sessions, then the rest in display order, skipping a chip that does not fit. What is left out is counted on the details button (`+N`, or an info icon). The server link, the Meridian Flip Monitor, the failures, the details button, and the first operation are never left out. An Abort is offered only while the event socket is online, and it sends the one command that interrupts that operation (`operationInterruption`, see Operation Lifecycle Rules): the `/stop` of its mount, focuser, rotator, dome, cover, or guide output, while the control store mirrors its connection and the driver reports it connected and able to abort; the abort of a capture, or its stop after the exposure on a camera that cannot abort; and the stop of the session of Guiding, Auto Focus, TPPA, DARV, Flat Wizard, Dark Library Wizard, Target Centering, or the Pointing Model Builder and its verification, found by exact operation id, which ends that session keeping what it did. A filter wheel change and an INDI property write have none. A motion of the profile's mount that no operation holds, reported by the driver as a goto, a manual axis motion, parking, or homing, shows as an operation of its own with its Abort. The click decides again from the live stores (`abortOperation`, `abortProfileMount`), so a profile, connection, or role switch after the render sends nothing; it interrupts that operation and nothing else, and shows a refused command as a device failure. While the socket is offline the operations shown are the last known, dimmed, without an Abort. The details button opens a panel above itself (`StatusDetailsPanel.tsx`, fixed in the viewport like the notification panel, closing on a press outside, Escape, a resize, or after opening something) with the server link and the selected connection, every role with its device link and status or full reading, the sessions with Open in Images while their camera tab is open, the enabled Meridian Flip Monitor of the mount with its status and Open tool, every other running operation with its devices, elapsed time (a timer of the open panel only), and reported progress, an Abort on each session and operation that has one, and the device failures. Closing the panel or navigating from it never stops a session or an operation; only an Abort does. The right group also shows the real-time clock of the profile, always the real current time, never the Sky simulation clock (`web/src/shell/statusbar/astronomicalclock.ts`): one or two readings of LAST, LMST, JD(UTC), MJD(UTC), UTC, or local time, chosen in Settings → Status Bar; the local time reads in the profile's display zone (Settings → Date & Time), and without an observing site JD(UTC) stands for a primary sidereal reading and a secondary one is left out. The clock is opportunistic: it is fitted after everything else (`fitClocks`), never counted on the details button and never `data-fixed`, so it changes nothing of how the chips and the operations are fitted, and it shows only while no chip and no operation is left out, in the room they leave. The server samples the time (`POST /api/astronomical-time/reference`, the same conversion path as `/convert`) on mount, on a new place of the active site, every 60 s, when the page is shown again, when the socket comes back, and when the wall and monotonic clocks of the tab disagree by 2 s, as after a sleep; between samples the readings move on by the monotonic time elapsed at the rate the server gives, which is the only sidereal arithmetic of the web, and a hidden page asks for nothing. A sidereal reading computed with UT1 taken as UTC starts with `~`, a reading 5 minutes old is dimmed and says so in its title, and from 30 minutes it shows `--`. A chip's title holds the full readout, and its click opens the Astronomical Time Converter on the real current time at the active site, whatever instant the tool held, and nothing else; the details panel has an Astronomical Time section with the same data and the same action. It is the only global status surface.
- **Modals**: Connections, Equipment Setup, and Profiles, over native `<dialog>` (`web/src/ui/Modal.tsx`).

Workspace frame (`WorkspaceLayout.tsx`), used by every workspace and every tool view:

- From top to bottom: optional `tabs` spanning the workspace (the Images document and camera tabs), a header at least 42 px tall (`min-h-12`) with the title, `context` controls, `actions`, and the panel toggle, whose actions wrap to a line of their own when they do not fit beside the title (the header then grows instead of clipping them), then the content beside the contextual panel. The content fills the remaining space and scrolls itself.
- The contextual panel (`ContextualPanel.tsx`) is the right-hand column of the current context: 280 px (`w-80`; 308 px, `w-88`, from `xl`), docked from Tailwind's `lg` breakpoint (`WIDE_SCREEN`, 64rem, 1024 px), its visibility a per-workspace preference of the profile; below `lg` it is a drawer over the content that starts closed, is never persisted, and closes with Escape or a click outside. A workspace whose own controls show the panel (the Sky toolbar's Framing, Display, and Location) holds this visibility with `useWorkspacePanel` and passes it as `panelState`, so a control that needs the panel opens it in either form. The workspace frame clips the closed drawer (`overflow: clip`) rather than hiding it, so no focus or scroll into view inside it can shift the workspace sideways. It holds no operational state, so collapsing or replacing it never stops what its controls started.
- Collapsible panel sections (`CollapsibleSection` of `web/src/shell/`): the heading is the toggle, with a one-line summary beside it while collapsed. Important sections start expanded, advanced or secondary ones collapsed (`defaultOpen`); a section the operator toggled keeps its state, keyed by its namespaced id (`sky.*`, `images.<tool>.*`), across selections, documents, camera tabs, and tool switches, and across reloads and tabs of the browser (`collapsibleSections` of `web/src/shell/collapsible.ts`, kept in browser storage by `web/src/profile/view.ts`). Every section of the Images tools, image tools and camera operations alike, is one, and a panel never nests a disclosure inside a section: an advanced group is a collapsed section of its own (for example Advanced Settings, Solver Details, Template Tags, or the Camera settings of Acquisition).
- Label and value rows (`DataRow` of `web/src/ui/DataRow.tsx`, grouped by `DataList`): every datum shown as a label and its value, in the contextual panels, the inspectors, Equipment Control, and the tool results, is one. Label and value share a line while both fit; a value that does not fit goes below its label, aligned to the end, and a label or value wider than the panel wraps instead of being cut. A value takes a tone (plain, success, or danger), never a layout of its own. Lists of another shape stay their own: the FITS header keywords, the filename template tags, and the metric tiles of the pointing diagnostics.
- The sections of the Sky Object Inspector, every object's and the Sun's and the Moon's, are collapsible sections, except the Object section, which holds the name, the type, the designations, the magnitude (the catalog's for a catalog object, the position's otherwise), and the actions: Show Framing, and Slew to Object (with Stop while slewing) only while the profile's mount is connected (`ObjectActions`); Position and Visibility start expanded and the others collapsed.
- One panel content at a time: Images shows the panel of the selected tool, Sky the selected panel view. Pages (Equipment Control, Settings) use a sidebar list and a detail area instead of a contextual panel.

Visual language (`web/src/index.css`, Tailwind 4 `@theme`):

- Dark theme only (`color-scheme: dark`), one font for all text: JetBrains Mono (`font-mono`).
- The root font is 14 px, so a rem is 14 px and a Tailwind spacing step (0.25rem) is 3.5 px: `h-12` is 42 px, `w-80` 280 px. Media queries ignore the root font and take the browser's 16 px rem, so the breakpoints keep Tailwind's widths (`lg` 1024 px, `xl` 1280 px). The radii are set in pixels and do not scale.
- Colour tokens, used as Tailwind utilities (`bg-base-100`, `text-ink-200`, `border-line-100`, …): surfaces `base-000` (darkest: rail, bars, panels, viewers) to `base-300` (hover); borders `line-100`, `line-200`; text `ink-100` (primary), `ink-200` (secondary), `ink-300` (muted); `accent`, `accent-hover`, `accent-subtle` (selected backgrounds), `on-accent`; `danger`, `success`, `warning`, `info`. A new colour is added as a token, never as a literal in a component.
- Radii `rounded-sm` (6 px), `rounded-md` (10 px), `rounded-lg` (16 px), `rounded-xl` (24 px). Panel headings are small uppercase `ink-200` text; dense panels use `text-xs`.
- Transitions honour `motion-reduce:transition-none`; focus is shown with an `accent` outline on `focus-visible`.

Components:

- Shared primitives live in `web/src/ui/`: `Button`, `IconButton` (icon only, its label is the tooltip; `pressed` for toggles), `Checkbox`, `RadioGroup`, `SegmentedControl` (two to five short options), `Select` (themed listbox), `TextInput`, `NumberInput`, `Field` (shared control look and unit suffixes such as `mm` or `°`), `Slider` (label, typed value, and track), `List`, `Modal` (with `ModalActions`, the row of its closing buttons), `ConfirmButton` (a command run only once a dialog confirms it), `NavigationMenu`, `Popover` (a toolbar button's floating panel of controls), `ContextMenu` (actions at a point, such as a right click on an image), `StatusBadge` (a state in words with a tone), `DataRow` and `DataList`, `Notice` (the muted line of explanation under a control or a section, or an error in the danger colour), `SectionHeading` (the small uppercase heading of a group), `Actions` (the wrapping row of a section's command buttons), `Table` (the data tables of the tools: a bordered frame that scrolls under a sticky header, columns of text or of numbers aligned to the end, and rows that select what they show), `ProgressBar` (the thin bar of a running exposure, delay, measurement, or calibration step, pulsing while the share done is unknown), `WorldMap` (the basemap of the eclipse and satellite maps, see World Map Contract), and `Icon` (the one icon set, 24×24 line paths, device types keyed by type). A new screen composes these; a new primitive is added there only when two features need it.
- Feature components are colocated with their feature (for example `web/src/camera/ExposureSettings.tsx`, or `EquipmentSetupButton` of `web/src/equipment/`, the offer of a panel that needs equipment); variants use `tv`, merging uses `cn`.
- Values are written by the semantic formatters of `web/src/format.ts`, one per quantity (degrees, arcminutes, signed arcminutes, arcseconds, angular sizes and signed offsets in the unit that suits them, signed hour angles in sidereal hours that never wrap to 24 h, geographic positions with hemispheres, temperatures, percentages, pixel lengths with their sky size, positions in an image, distances in kilometers, exposure times as set, durations, running clocks, signed running clocks, instants in UTC with the zone named, byte sizes in binary units, wide-range measurements), each taking the value in its computational unit and returning text only; `MISSING` ("—") is the one text of an absent value, and a value that is not finite is shown as it, never as NaN. Sky coordinates are written by `web/src/sky/format.ts` (with the apparent diameters and fields of view of the Sky, in arcminutes as catalogs give them, distances, magnitudes, and local times) and, in the profile's coordinate format, by `web/src/platesolving/coordinates.ts` (with position angles and pixel scales). A unit or a number of decimals is chosen after rounding, so a boundary value carries (59.999″ reads 1.00′, and a right ascension or position angle that rounds to a full turn reads 0), while a declination keeps its sign when it rounds to zero.
- Every pixel image is shown by `ImageViewer` (`web/src/viewer/`) with overlays in image coordinates (see Overlay Contracts); the star map is the canvas renderer of `web/src/sky/celestial/`.

Behaviour every screen keeps:

- Status is always text, colour only reinforces it; absence is shown as absence, never as 0.
- Values shown are those the server reports; inputs hold pending values until an explicit action (Apply, Save, Start, Solve Image, …).
- Unavailable actions stay visible and disabled with the reason as their tooltip (`Not available yet.`, the busy or missing device); a panel that needs equipment says what to set up and offers Equipment Setup.
- Lists, table rows that select, rails, tabs, and carousels move with the arrow keys, Home, and End (`moveIndex` in `web/src/ui/navigation.ts`) and select with Enter or Space. Native elements, `title`, and visually hidden text name controls; `aria-*` attributes are not used (`AGENTS.md`).
- Layouts work from phone width up: secondary text hides with `max-sm:`, the panel becomes a drawer, toolbars wrap (the Sky search takes a line of its own, with the buttons below it), and nothing scrolls the page horizontally.

## Shared Image Contracts

There is **one** image store, `ImageStore` in `api/src/images.ts`, and the scientific image reference is its document id.

- An image document is addressed as `/api/images/:id/...` by every operation, whatever workspace requested it. There is no second solver or detector path for camera frames.
- Camera frames enter the store as documents of the origin `{ origin: 'capture', connectionId, deviceId, frameId, time, path? }` (`ImageStore.openCapture`), decoded from the bytes the driver sent. A frame is never identified by camera role.
- In loop capture, each frame is its own document. An analysis stays bound to the id it was started on, and a newer frame never changes it. The capture frame registry (`api/src/captureframes.ts`) keeps the latest frame of each camera open and closes the previous one unless a lease of the Images workspace holds it (see Capture Frames Contract). An analysis retains its document (`ImageStore.retain`/`release`): closing it hides it from every route at once, but its pixels are released only when the last analysis lets it go.
- A derived document is computed from another document and never changes it. A live stack snapshot is a derived document too, of provenance `liveStacking` (its session, what it shows, the accepted frames it integrates, and its stacks as they were when it was taken), adopted from a copy of the stacks (see Live Stacking Contract). Its provenance (`processing`) records the steps of the image processing pipeline that computed it (see Image Processing Contract), whose result the store adopts (`ImageStore.adopt`) from a full copy it handed out (`ImageStore.processingImage`). It is floating point, keeps the min-max rescale of the source read so a save writes the scale of the source, and records its source, time, and what was applied in HISTORY.
- Analysis jobs, common to star detection, streak detection, annotation, and plate solving, and to the scheduling of image processing: an in-memory queue (`AnalysisScheduler` of `api/src/analysis.ts`) run in arrival order under a concurrency limit per provider, at most 16 jobs waiting (`MAX_QUEUED`; beyond it a submission is `503 queueFull`), each job retaining its image until it ends. Completed results are kept in memory only, at most 8 per image and 256 in all, dropped when their image is released, and never survive a restart; a result no longer kept answers `404 expired`. An equivalent request (the parameters each contract names) is answered with its kept result (200) instead of running again. An analysis `<x>` is submitted by `POST /api/images/:id/<x>` (202 with the job; `404 notFound` for an image that is not open), lists the kept completed jobs of an image with `GET /api/images/:id/<x>`, and answers the snapshot of the jobs waiting, running, and recently ended with `GET /api/<x>`, one job with `GET /api/<x>/:jobId`, the full result with `GET /api/<x>/:jobId/result`, and cancels with `POST /api/<x>/:jobId/cancel` (204, or 404 when it is not waiting or running). Every change of a job is published as `<x>:updated` without its payload, which is fetched on demand. No device is held. The web stores of star detection, streak detection, plate solving, and annotation mirror their jobs through `trackJob` of `web/src/analysis.ts`: the latest job of each image, a bounded set of fetched results, and a job state that only moves forward, so a snapshot or a submission answer arriving after the events of a job never takes an ended job back.
- An analysis of one spot of an image reads a region instead of the whole image: `ImageStore.detectionRegion` answers the grayscale copy of a rectangle, sample for sample the same as that rectangle of `detectionImage` (a mosaic is debayered on the rectangle plus one pixel around it, with its pattern shifted to that origin), without copying the rest of the image, and the `SampleConversion` back to the scale of the source: ADU for integer data, or the unit of the source when the data is floating point or has a `BSCALE` other than 1, undoing the min-max rescale of the read.
- A Sky Framing survey image is not an image document. Importing one into Images is an explicit action, available only when the cutout carries usable WCS metadata.

## Capture Frames Contract

- `CaptureFrameRegistry` (`api/src/captureframes.ts`) indexes the frames of Camera Capture, Auto Focus, TPPA, Mount Pointing, Target Centering, DARV, Flat Wizard, Dark Library Wizard, and Guiding by physical camera (connection id + device id, never a role, profile, tool, or operation). A frame is identified by its `frameId` (`sessionId:generation`, the session that took it and the frame number within it), the acquisition, and its `origin` (`capture`, `autofocus`, `tppa`, `mountPointing`, `targetCentering`, `darv`, `flatWizard`, `darkLibraryWizard`, or `guiding`) names the operation that took it; its `imageId` is only the document currently holding its pixels, and a saved frame reopened from its file gets a new one. Frames are ordered by a registry-wide `sequence`, never by time, and every change carries a `revision` so a late event never replaces a newer state. Every producer shares one latest frame per camera, ordered by the order the registry received them, never by their session numbering, and a write completing late never takes the latest frame back. Auto Focus records every sampled and verification frame it decoded, before it is measured and whether or not an HFD could be measured; its files are auxiliary and deleted, so its frames are never saved, and only become the latest frame and then transient frames. TPPA records its poses and refinement frames the same way, before they are solved, Mount Pointing each sample frame, Target Centering each frame it measures, and DARV the frame of each measurement, before it is analyzed. Guiding records only the newest guide frame it previewed, which is never saved. Flat Wizard records every trial and final frame from its staging file before it is measured; an accepted frame becomes saved through `published()`, the only change of a frame's path, which names the final file it was published under (a frame the registry does not know is recorded from that file), while trial and rejected frames stay previewed only and become transient. Dark Library Wizard does the same with every frame it examines, an accepted frame becoming saved through `published()` once it was committed to its plan; the 20 saved frames are only a recent history, and the plan is the ledger of a run. `written()` only records the outcome of a write to the path a frame already names.
- Each camera keeps two collections with separate retention: its **latest** frame, the newest one the preview decoded since the server started (previewed only, being saved, or saved), and its **saved** frames, those whose file was written, the only history, of which the newest `FRAMES_PER_SOURCE` (20) are listed first and older ones by pages. A frame joins the saved frames only once its write succeeds (a failed write never does), so any number of previewed-only frames never evicts a saved one, and a previewed-only latest frame takes no saved slot; a saved latest frame is also the newest saved frame. A former latest frame that was not saved is kept in memory as a **transient** frame (at most 20 per camera), never listed nor persisted, only so its state can still be asked for.
- The registry never holds pixels. A frame is `memory` while its document is open, `file` when its saved file exists (opened as a new `file` document on demand), `saving` while its file is written and its document closed, or `unavailable` (`expired`, `fileMissing`, `saveFailed`, `restarted`). A saved frame the preview skipped is recorded from its file; a skipped frame that was only previewed is not. Opening never substitutes another frame.
- The registry owns the closing of capture documents, Auto Focus frames included: the latest previewed frame of each camera stays open, an older one only while a lease holds it. `POST /api/capture-frames/frames/:frameId/open` leases the document for a client (the id a page opens `/api/ws?client=<id>` with, `clientId` of `web/src/events.ts`, generated on load since Images documents do not survive a reload); `DELETE /api/capture-frames/leases/:lease` releases it. At most `MAX_LEASED_FRAMES` (16) documents are kept open by leases besides the latest frames; beyond that an open is refused with `limit`. Refusals carry the codes `notFound`, `expired`, `fileMissing`, `saveFailed`, `saving`, `limit`, and `unreadable`.
- When the last socket of a client closes, its leases are released after `LEASE_GRACE` (10 min), so a phone whose screen locked finds its frames again; a tab that reopens its socket sends the leases it still uses to `PUT /api/capture-frames/clients/:client/leases`, which releases the others and answers the lost ones; a page that unloads releases all of its leases with a beacon (`POST` on the same route).
- `GET /api/capture-frames` is the snapshot of the cameras (with an `epoch` per server run): each camera gives its `latestFrameId`, its `latestAvailableFrameId` (the latest frame, or the newest saved frame whose file exists when the latest cannot be opened or there is none), `latestRestored` (no latest frame since the server started), and `count` of saved frames, every row the database keeps for it. `GET /api/capture-frames/sources/:connectionId/:deviceId` gives a page of the saved `frames`, newest first, empty for a camera without frames: the first one with the `latest` frame and the saved frames the registry remembers, and each later one, asked with `?before=` the `next` cursor of the previous page (`&limit=`, 20 by default, at most `MAX_FRAME_PAGE` = 200), the older saved frames read from the database; `next` is absent on the last page. An older saved frame listed or opened is kept in memory like the others, at most 200 per camera besides those with an open document, so it opens, leases, and reports its state the same way. `GET /api/capture-frames/frames/:frameId` gives one frame (latest, saved, transient, or older saved). Changes are published as `captureFrame:updated` (frame and camera) and `captureSource:updated` (connection state); neither carries pixels or server paths, only file names. A disconnected camera keeps its frames.
- The metadata of every saved frame is a row of `capture_frames` in the operational database (identity, origin, acquisition, size, and path, never pixels), written in the same step that makes the frame saved and deleted when its write is later reported failed; previewed-only frames have no row. The registry-wide `sequence` continues from the highest row. Rows are never pruned and no file is ever deleted with them; a row that cannot be written is reported on the console and never fails the capture. After a restart no frame is in memory and no camera has a latest frame: the newest 20 saved frames of each camera are read again, available when their file still exists, and previewed-only ones are gone. No directory is scanned.
- Images has a permanent **camera tab** for each connected camera of the profile's watched connection (`deviceState` of `web/src/equipment/equipment.ts`, `reconcileCameraTabs` in `web/src/images/cameratabs.ts`), keyed by connection id + device id, ordered by the role of the camera in the equipment setup of the active profile (the Main Camera, then the Guide Camera, then the others) and then by camera name and id, before the closable file and derived tabs; cameras of the same name get the start of their id. It is created and removed with the camera, never closed by the operator (`isClosable`: no close button, middle click, or Delete), and switching profile or connection changes the camera tabs.
