A blind solve sends no position but keeps a known scale, and its FITS copy leaves the pointing keywords out so ASTAP cannot use them. The current mount position is never a hint; a mount pointing session hints the position it commanded, explicitly. Every job keeps the hints it used with their origins. Angles are radians; the scale is radians per pixel.
- A result keeps the whole WCS header of the solver as the toolkit's `PlateSolution` (ICRS center, scale, orientation, parity, field size). It is accepted only when `plateSolutionFrom` proves it (equatorial TAN or TAN-SIP, ICRS or FK5 J2000) and its image size is the document's, within 1 px (ASTAP rebuilds NAXIS from CRPIX); otherwise the job fails as `invalidSolution`. A solver that ended normally without a WCS completes with `outcome: noSolution`, which is not a failure. The WCS a header already had is reported apart (`GET /api/images/:id/plate-solutions`, `header`) and never replaced; no file is written.
- Jobs are the analysis jobs of the Shared Image Contracts, with one ASTAP and one solve-field process and two nova solves at a time. An equivalent request (image, provider, strategy, effective hints, tolerance, downsampling, provider settings) is answered with the job still waiting or running, or with its kept result (200 for both), so a double click, a second tab, or a retry never solves twice. The service itself never syncs, slews, or centers a mount; Target Centering does that with what it measures.
- Nova (`api/src/platesolving.nova.ts`) uploads the FITS copy once, as a private submission, publishes the stages `uploading`, `submitted` (submission id), `solving` (job id), and `retrieving`, polls every 5 s, and gives up after 3 failed requests in a row (`network`) or at the provider timeout. An upload refused for its session is `sessionExpired`. Cancel stops following the remote job only, and says so. The following of a nova job does not survive a restart, and nothing is uploaded again.
- Routes are `POST /api/images/:id/plate-solving` (`422 notConfigured` when the provider is not configured) and `/api/plate-solving`, and the event `plateSolving:updated` without the WCS; instead of a list of kept jobs, `GET /api/images/:id/plate-solutions` gives the header's WCS and the kept completed jobs, and `GET /api/images/:id/plate-solving/hints?strategy=` the hints an image would use.
- Images → Plate Solver (`web/src/images/PlateSolverPanel.tsx`, store `web/src/platesolving/platesolving.ts`) edits the document's hints and the settings the profile shares between the documents (`imagePlateSolving`), and sends them only with Solve Image; on a camera tab it edits the plate solving settings of the physical camera instead (`CameraSettings.plateSolving`, `effectivePlateSolveRequest`), which TPPA and the other operations of that camera apply, keeping only the hints in the document, so a typed position never reaches an operation; the request is editable before the document has an image (`imageUnavailableReason` says why), with Solve Image disabled until it has one. The solution shown is the latest submitted job of the image unless the operator chose another one or the header's (`ImagePlateSolvingState.selected`), so a job that ends never replaces a chosen solution; the choice is offered whenever the image has a header solution, even before any solve, or more than one completed job (`solutionOptions`), and a header solution is never shown unless chosen; results are fetched only for the image shown. Zoom, pan, stretch, and the overlay toggles never solve. The solution shows the position angle of the image top (`imageTopPositionAngle`, the sky direction of the top edge as the viewer draws it, at the reference pixel), the same quantity as the Rotation of Sky Framing; `solution.orientation` is the solver’s own angle and is not shown.
- Operation integration: `PlateSolvingService.solve(imageId, request, signal, origin)` resolves to the result of that image (a search without a solution completes with `outcome: noSolution`), whatever newer frame arrives, cancels with its signal, and throws `PlateSolvingError` with the job code otherwise; a preparation resolved once keeps the provider settings of a whole session. TPPA jobs carry the `tppa` origin (session, camera, frame id, and the pose during acquisition), mount pointing jobs the `mountPointing` origin, and Target Centering jobs the `targetCentering` origin (session, camera, frame id).
- Sync Mount (the Mount section of Images → Plate Solver, shown with a solution) syncs the MOUNT of the active equipment setup on the centre of the solution shown, after a confirmation, through `POST /api/connections/:id/mounts/:deviceId/sync`: the ICRS centre is sent as J2000 in decimal hours and degrees (`mountSyncTarget`). It is offered only while the mount is connected, can sync, is not parked, and no operation or command holds it (`mountSyncBlocker`); the server checks again. This action only syncs: it never slews or centers the mount.
- Coordinates read from a solution are in its own frame: `wcsFrame` (`web/src/platesolving/wcs.ts`) names it `ICRS` or `FK5 J2000` from RADESYS, else EQUINOX, and every label shows it; the toolkit takes FK5 J2000 as the ICRS (within 0.03″) but the UI never relabels it. Separations and position angles (`skyOffset`) use the toolkit's atan2 formulas (`sphericalSeparation`, `sphericalPositionAngle`), from north through east, undefined for coincident or antipodal positions.
- Measurements and markers (`web/src/platesolving/measurement.ts`, state `ImageViewState.astrometry`, tool `web/src/images/astrometryTool.ts`) are kept per document as points in viewer pixels, kept inside the image, never as sky positions, in tab memory only: their values are computed again from the selected solution, so choosing another one keeps what was pointed at, and a point it cannot unproject makes them unavailable. A camera tab clears them when it shows another frame. A distance reports its great-circle separation, the position angle from north through east, ΔRA and ΔDec (a difference of right ascension, not an angle on the sky), the gnomonic east and north offsets of its end around its start, and its length in pixels, and is drawn along the great circle through the WCS. A rectangle is axis-aligned in image pixels, not in right ascension and declination: it reports the great-circle lengths of its four sides, of its two centre lines (between the midpoints of opposite sides), and of its diagonals, and its size in pixels, since one width would mislead under distortion. A circle has a constant angular radius on the sky around its centre, set by a rim point: its outline is that small circle sampled at every position angle (48 to 360 vertices, about one per 6 screen pixels) and projected through the WCS, so it may look elliptical, and it reports its radius, diameter, and centre; a degenerate or unprojectable one is unavailable. A marker is a fixed point with its coordinates in the WCS frame and its zero-based pixel. The measure mode of the viewer toolbar (per document, Pan by default, disabled without a solution or while Guiding picks a star) owns every press: a drag draws, or a click then a second click, and a click places a marker; with the Plate Solver tool shown, presses on a handle (12 px: the points of a distance or circle, the corners and edge midpoints of a rectangle) or a marker drag it in any mode and a click selects the item whose outline is under it, the rest pans. The gesture in progress lives in its own store, so drawing renders only its overlay; Escape drops it, then leaves the mode, then clears the selection, and Delete removes the selected item. The Measurements and Markers sections of the panel list them with their values, and rename, hide, delete, and clear them.
- The context menu of a solved image (`web/src/images/ImageMenu.tsx` over the generic `ContextMenu` of `web/src/ui/`, opened by a right click or a long press on a point inside the image; elsewhere the browser's own menu is kept) copies the point's right ascension and declination with the frame's name, or its zero-based pixel, adds a marker there, or starts a distance from it (Measure From Here); the drawing actions are disabled while Guiding picks a star. Every action uses the point the menu opened on. Copies go through `copyText` (`web/src/clipboard.ts`): the Clipboard API where the context is secure, otherwise a selection copied by the browser, since the app is usually opened over plain HTTP on the local network; a copy that fails keeps the menu open and says so.
- Target Offset (`web/src/images/TargetOffsetSection.tsx`, drawn by `web/src/images/TargetOffsetOverlay.tsx`, values `targetOffset` in `web/src/platesolving/measurement.ts`) is read only: it reports the offset of one target per document from the solved centre of the image (the separation, the position angle from north through east, and the east and north offsets on the plane tangent at the centre), and where the target falls in the image. The target is a marker of the image (located with the selected solution), a right ascension and declination typed in the frame of the solution, or the catalog star or deep-sky object selected in Sky, taken at its J2000 catalog position, epoch J2000.0, without proper motion; solar-system bodies, minor bodies, constellations, and the mount are refused with the reason. Inside the image the target is a ring with a dashed line from the centre; outside it, an arrow at the border along the direction towards it, labelled with the separation. Its values follow the selected solution; a marker target is dropped with its marker or when a camera tab shows another frame (which clears the markers), while a typed or Sky target is kept across frames. It never changes the Sky selection and nothing is sent to a mount.
- Limitations: only TAN and TAN-SIP solutions in the ICRS or FK5 J2000 are accepted; ASTAP may not write SIP terms; solve-field applies its own ±30% scale window and only nova takes `scaleTolerance`; ASTAP's "not enough stars" (exit code 2) is reported as a search without a solution.

## Image Processing Contract

- One service, `ImageProcessingService` (`api/src/imageprocessing.ts`), applies a pipeline to an image document: an ordered list of single-image operations of the toolkit (`ImageProcessingOperation`): debayer, automatic background extraction, background neutralization, arcsinh stretch, multiscale linear and median transforms, Gaussian blur, sharpen, cosmetic correction, horizontal and vertical flips, and grayscale. A request carries only the enabled steps, in order, each with the id the editor gave it; the job keeps them as an immutable snapshot. Parameters are the toolkit's own, which clamps them; they are not validated.
- What a pipeline can apply to an image is checked when it is submitted (`checkPipeline`), from the channels and trusted CFA pattern of the source, following what each step makes of them: debayer needs a mono image and a pattern (the trusted one for `auto`) and makes RGB, grayscale makes mono, background neutralization needs RGB, and a raw CFA mosaic takes only debayer, cosmetic correction (by colour phase), and the flips (which keep the pattern in phase). A refusal is `422 incompatibleImage` with the `stepId`; no operation is skipped or converted silently.
- The source is never changed: the image worker hands a full copy (`ImageStore.processingImage`, a trusted pattern named at the image origin) to a worker of its own (`api/src/imageprocessing.worker.ts`), which applies the steps in order, announcing each one, and transfers the result back. Only when every step succeeded is the result adopted as **one** derived document (`ImageStore.adopt`, provenance `processing`); a failure names its step (`errorStepId`, `processingFailed`), and a failed or cancelled job adopts nothing, even when the cancel arrives during the adoption.
- Scheduling is the analysis queue of the Shared Image Contracts (arrival order, at most 16 waiting, the source retained until the job ends), with one pipeline at a time and at most one waiting or running pipeline per source image (a second one is `409 busy` with the job). A running pipeline is cancelled, or stopped after 10 min (`timeout`), by terminating its worker. Results are never reused: running again creates another document.
- Jobs are published as `imageProcessing:updated` (state, `currentStepIndex`, `completedSteps`, `outputImageId`, the failure), never with pixels or paths; `GET /api/image-processing` is the snapshot, `GET /api/image-processing/:jobId` one job, `POST /api/images/:id/processing` submits (202), and `POST /api/image-processing/:jobId/cancel` cancels; there is no result route.
- The output document of a completed job is handed to a client by `POST /api/image-processing/:jobId/claim`, which answers its `ImageInfo` (again for a repeated claim while it is open; `expired` once closed, `invalidState` before completion). A document never claimed within `CLAIM_GRACE` (10 min) is closed, so a tab that disappeared leaves no orphan; a claimed one follows the lifecycle of every document.
- Images → Image Processing (`web/src/images/ImageProcessingPanel.tsx`, `ProcessingStepCard.tsx`, `ProcessingStepForms.tsx`) edits the pipeline of the document (`ImageProcessingDraft` in its view state, so a camera tab keeps it across frames and a reconnection of its camera, started from the pipeline of the profile, `ProfileSettings.imageProcessing`, which every edit of the steps replaces; see Persistence and State) as cards that are added from a grouped selector (`imageprocessing.catalog.ts`), reordered by dragging their handle or with Move and Alt+↑/↓, enabled or disabled (a disabled step keeps its parameters and is never sent), expanded independently of that, edited, duplicated (new id), and removed. Run Pipeline sends a deep copy of the enabled steps, so edits during a run apply to the next one; it is disabled, with the reason, without an image, without an enabled step, while a pipeline of the document runs, or when a step cannot apply (`pipelineIssue`, the same rules as `checkPipeline`), whose card shows why, as it shows a refusal or failure naming it. A camera tab runs on the frame it shows and keeps following the camera.
- The mirror (`web/src/images/imageprocessing.ts`, in `SERVER_STORES`) never lets a late event take a job back to an earlier state or fewer finished steps. A tab claims only the jobs it submitted, once, on the event or the snapshot that shows them completed (or on the answer of a submission whose job already completed); the output opens as an unsaved derived document, the active one only when its source still is. Stop Processing waits for the server to report the end.
- Limitations: no preview, no memory budget beyond the size of the image (a pipeline holds several copies of it while it runs); jobs do not survive a restart, and pipelines being edited do not survive a reload; XISF stores the HISTORY of a derived image as one keyword.

## Auto Focus Contract

- `AutoFocusSessions` (`api/src/autofocus.ts`) runs the toolkit's `runAutoFocus` for one camera and one focuser inside a single `focuser.autofocus` operation, which holds both devices for the whole session; the runner nests its focuser lease and the per-frame camera leases in it. The runner owns the sampling, the fitting (Hyperbolic, Parabolic, Trend Lines, and the two combined modes), and the best position; nothing is fitted in `api` or `web`.
- The service supplies the runner with the backlash compensated mover of `FocuserCommands` (plus an optional settle wait), the `CameraCapturer` of the capture service (one FITS frame per position, written to a temporary directory and deleted after it was measured, never saved as a capture), and `StarDetectionService.measure`, whose median HFD of the accepted stars (Multi-Star) is each measurement. The provider and its ASTAP settings are fixed when the session starts; a failed detection is an invalid point of the curve, never a fallback to the other provider.
- The frames take the exposure time of the Auto Focus form (`AutoFocusSettings.exposureTime`) and the camera settings (gain, offset, binning, subframe, readout mode) of the physical camera shared with Camera Acquisition (`CameraSettings.exposure`, whose exposure time is never sent), fitted to the camera's capabilities by `fitExposure` and snapshotted in the request, so editing them never changes a running session; the Auto Focus panel edits the exposure time and the routine, never the camera settings. The frame type is always LIGHT, the stars are measured with the profile's star detection request (see Star Detection Contract), and every frame snoops the focuser of the session and the mount, filter wheel, and rotator of the equipment setup (`AutoFocusRequest.snoop`).
- After the runner moved the focuser to the best focus, one more frame there records the verified HFD, kept apart from the HFD the curve estimated; a session completes only after it. A rejected curve fails without a best focus, and the runner moves the focuser back to the start. A stop, or an Equipment Control stop of the focuser (which cancels the operation holding it), cancels where the session stands. When the request sets `restoreOnStop` (the form's default), a stop of `POST /api/autofocus/:sessionId/stop` then moves the focuser back to the start position in an operation of its own, once the session released it and only if it is free (`restoring`, then `cancelled` with `restored`); a second stop cancels that move where it stands, and an Equipment Control stop or a disconnection never moves the focuser back. While a frame is exposed, the summary carries the driver's countdown as `exposure` (microseconds and percent), cleared once the frame was read.
- Sessions live in memory, the latest of each camera: `GET /api/autofocus` is the snapshot of their summaries, `GET /api/autofocus/:sessionId` the complete record with its measurements (requested and observed position, image id, HFD, star counts) and its curve, sampled from the runner's own regressions (`api/src/autofocus.curve.ts`), and `autofocus:updated` carries every change. Each sampled and verification frame is recorded in the capture frame registry before it is measured (origin `autofocus`, the verification numbered after the samples; see Capture Frames Contract), whose closing the registry owns, so a measurement's image id stays open only while it is the latest frame of its camera or leased; the measurement job retains the document until it ends.
- `POST /api/connections/:id/cameras/:deviceId/autofocus` starts a session (202 with `sessionId` and `operationId`; a repeated token answers its session), refused with 404 for an unknown camera or focuser, 409 when either is disconnected or held, and 422 when the focuser cannot move to absolute positions or the detector is ASTAP and it is not configured. The route answers through `admit(connectionId, deviceId, request, invocation?)`, the start of a caller inside the process, which answers that `CommandRefusal` or the session and operation ids, `repeated`, and `ended`, the final summary once the session released its devices (after the move back of a stop). Under an invocation (see Operation Lifecycle Rules), the camera and the focuser must be reserved by it, and every operation of the session, the move back included, runs inside the reservation; a move back after the reservation was cancelled is refused, and the focuser stays where it stands. `interrupt(sessionId)` is the in-process stop. A token repeated after a newer session of the camera replaced its own still answers its own session, which `CameraSessionRegistry` remembers by operation (the last 256); the snapshot, the routes of a session id, and the events name only the latest.
- Limitations: focusers that only move relatively are refused; there is one frame per position and no repeated samples; the runner's sweep starts `initialOffsetSteps` steps out and comes back one step past the start before extending each side, so it is not symmetric; the filter wheel is never moved; the mount is not held, because the arbiter has no shared hold that would refuse GoTo and Nudge while letting tracking and guide pulses run, so a slew during a session goes unnoticed; sessions do not survive a restart. The simulated camera defocuses relative to its ±50000 step travel, so its curves need step sizes in the thousands.

## TPPA Contract

- `TppaSessions` (`api/src/tppa.ts`) runs the toolkit's `acquireThreePointPolarAlignment` and `refineThreePointPolarAlignment` for one camera and one mount inside a single `mount.tppa` operation, which holds both devices from the first frame until the session ends, refinement and pauses included, so no GoTo, nudge, capture, or Auto Focus can come between the poses or the refinement frames. The runner owns the RA motions, the acceptance of each pose, the geometry check (`core/src/observation/alignment/tppa.geometry.ts`), and the alignment; nothing is fitted in `api` or `web`.
- The operator chooses the RA direction (`WEST` or `EAST`), a slew-rate preset the mount announces (applied and confirmed by its echo before any motion), the RA rotation aimed at between poses (radians; the form keeps degrees, 6° to 15°), the settle delay, and the refinement delay. The motions are timed manual motions of the mount commander shared with Mount Nudge (`MountCommands.commander`): the first lasts 1 s, motions without measurable rotation grow fourfold up to 60 s, and once the mount rotated they are sized from the rotation observed per second; each one is stopped in a `finally`, and the stop waits for the mount to be quiescent, escalating to the abort. The rotation is measured from the solves (the change of hour angle about the celestial pole), never assumed from the motion time.
- A pose is accepted at 6° of rotation since the previous one, at most 30° from the first, with at least 1° of sky separation; a reversed motion, a declination change over 2°, a field within 10° of the pole (the first pose included, before any motion), a pier-side or tracking change, 12 captures without the pose, or an ill-conditioned triple fail the session as `unexpectedState`, never with an estimate. The seed pole error is about 2.4·σ/φ² for a solve error σ and a step φ, whatever the declination, which is why the limits are rotations.
- Frames take the exposure time of the TPPA form (`TppaSettings.exposureTime`) and the camera settings of the physical camera shared with Camera Acquisition (`CameraSettings.exposure`, whose exposure time is never sent), snapshotted in the request, as one LIGHT FITS frame each, with the mount snooped into the header; files are auxiliary, in a temporary directory deleted with the session. Each frame is recorded in the capture frame registry (origin `tppa`; see Capture Frames Contract) before it is solved with `PlateSolvingService.solve` (origin `tppa`), whose provider and settings are resolved once when the session starts and never fall back to another. A frame without a solution is a warning: during acquisition the mount moves further, during refinement three in a row fail the session.
- After the third pose the session is `readyToRefine`. `POST /api/tppa/:sessionId/refinement/start` starts (or resumes) the refinement, which captures and solves every refinement delay and feeds `ThreePointPolarAlignment`, never commanding the mount; `…/refinement/stop` pauses it (`refinementPaused`), keeping the devices. A field that moved more than 5° on the sky between refinement frames means the mount slewed and fails the session. Every estimate carries the azimuth, altitude, and total errors and the knob changes inferred, and the overlay of its frame computed by `computeThreePointPolarAlignmentOverlay` with the center of the third pose as the persistent reference.
- `POST /api/tppa/:sessionId/stop` cancels the session wherever it stands (an Equipment Control stop of either device, or a disconnection, does too): `completed` once it had an estimate, `cancelled` before. When the operation ended, the preset it replaced is restored if the mount is free, reported as `slewRateRestored`, and, when the request sets `stopTracking`, the tracking it started is stopped, reported as `trackingStopped`, each with a warning when it could not be; with `stopTracking` false the tracking is left on.
- `POST /api/connections/:id/cameras/:deviceId/tppa` starts a session (202 with `sessionId` and `operationId`; a repeated token answers the same session), refused with 404 for an unknown camera or mount, 409 when either is disconnected or held, and 422 when the solver is not configured or the mount is not equatorial, cannot move west-east or abort, lacks the preset, reports no site (0, 0), is parked, or is not tracking and cannot switch tracking. A mount that is not tracking but can switch it has its tracking started inside the operation before the first frame (`trackingStarted`); one that reports track modes and tracks at another rate than sidereal is not refused, but the runner fails the session before any motion. Sessions live in memory, the latest of each camera: `GET /api/tppa` and `GET /api/tppa/:sessionId` are the snapshot and the complete record (poses, attempts, initial estimate, overlay), and `tppa:updated` carries every change. TPPA has no in-process admission: it runs only as a manual operation, never under a reservation. While a frame is exposed, the summary carries the driver's countdown as `exposure` (microseconds and percent), cleared once the frame is solved, through the `captureListener` the toolkit runner gives to every auxiliary capture (`exposureProgressListener` of `api/src/captures.ts`).
- Limitations: the backend knows no obstacle, cable, or mechanical limit the driver does not report, so the direction is the operator's responsibility; guiding or dithering by another program during the refinement goes unnoticed; refraction uses the default atmosphere; only the mount is snooped into the frames; sessions do not survive a restart.

## DARV Contract

- `DarvSessions` (`api/src/darv.ts`) runs drift alignment for one camera and one mount as a session of measurements. Each measurement is its own `mount.darv` operation, which holds both devices only while the toolkit's `runDarv` (`core/src/observation/alignment/darv.runner.ts`) exposes one frame, so the operator turns the knobs between measurements with the devices free. The runner owns the slew rate, the timing, and the motions; nothing is timed in `api` or `web`, and nothing is plate solved.
- The operator chooses the axis being adjusted (`azimuth` or `altitude`, which only tells where to point), the direction of the first RA motion (`EAST` or `WEST`), a slew-rate preset the mount announces (applied and confirmed by its echo before the exposure), and the leg duration (the form keeps 5 s to 600 s). The exposure is `darvExposureTime`: both legs plus a 3 s lead with the stars still, a 3 s turn budget, and a 3 s trailing margin. Once the exposure is seen Busy the runner waits the lead, moves out for the leg duration, stops, moves back for the same time, and stops; each motion is a manual motion of the commander shared with Mount Nudge, stopped in a `finally` that waits for the mount to be quiescent. A return motion that ended later than the earliest possible end of the shutter (dispatch plus exposure) fails the measurement instead of keeping a truncated trail; a mount that disconnects or stops tracking between legs fails it too.
- The timing measured on the monotonic clock shared with the capturer (lead, outbound, turn, return, trailing, adding up to the exposure) is kept with the measurement and given to the analysis, which excludes the stationary lead and trail from the drift baseline.
- The frame takes the camera settings shared with Camera Acquisition (`CameraSettings.exposure`) without its exposure time, as one LIGHT FITS frame with the mount snooped into the header; the file is auxiliary, in a temporary directory deleted with the measurement. The frame is recorded in the capture frame registry (origin `darv`; see Capture Frames Contract) before the devices are released.
- A measurement starts the tracking of a mount that is not tracking inside its operation, before the runner, and the session records it (`trackingStarted`); the tracking stays on between measurements, and an open session replaced on the same mount hands it to the new one. `POST /api/darv/:sessionId/finish` takes `{ stopTracking }` (the panel's "Stop the tracking when finishing" checkbox, on by default) and, when set, stops that tracking before the session is published as finished, if the mount is free (`trackingStopped`); a repeat or another finish meanwhile is refused with 409. Once the operation of a measurement ended, the preset it replaced is restored if the mount is free (`slewRateRestored`), and the frame, retained in the image store, is analyzed by `DarvAnalyzer` (`api/src/darv.analysis.ts`): `analyzeDarvImage` on a worker of its own (`api/src/darv.worker.ts`, embedded by `app/build.ts`), one analysis at a time, cancelled by terminating the worker. There is no sky transform, so the drift is the unsigned separation rate of the legs across the RA direction, in pixels per second, never a polar error. The outcome is `measured` (a drift from unclipped, resolved trails), `limited` (only clipped, saturated, or unresolved trails; no drift is published, never a zero), or `inconclusive` (no pair of legs), with the toolkit's diagnostics and a fit quality that is not a probability. Drifts are comparable only between measurements of equal settings.
- Measurement states: `preparing`, `exposing`, `outbound`, `reversing`, `inbound`, `finishing`, `analyzing`, then `ready`, `failed`, or `aborted`; `ready` says the measurement ended, not that the mount is aligned. Session states: `measuring`, `idle`, `finished`.
- `POST /api/connections/:id/cameras/:deviceId/darv` starts a session and its first measurement (replacing an idle or finished session of the camera), `POST /api/darv/:sessionId/measurements` repeats one with new settings (both 202 with `sessionId`, `operationId`, and `generation`), `POST /api/darv/:sessionId/abort` aborts the measurement wherever it stands (stopping the mount and discarding the exposure, or cancelling the analysis; an Equipment Control stop or a disconnection aborts it too), and `POST /api/darv/:sessionId/finish` finishes an idle session. Starts take a token, so a repeated request answers the same measurement; they are refused with 404 for an unknown camera or mount (or session), 422 when the mount is not equatorial, cannot move west-east or abort, lacks the preset, is parked, is not tracking and cannot switch tracking, or tracks at another rate than sidereal, and 409 while the camera's session measures or finishes or a device is disconnected or held.
- Sessions live in memory, the latest of each camera, each with its latest 50 measurements: `GET /api/darv` and `GET /api/darv/:sessionId` are the snapshot and the complete record (the paired trails of each analyzed measurement, in 0-based pixels), and `darv:updated` carries every change.
- Limitations: with equal legs the order of the outer ends is not known, so the drift has no sign and does not say which way to turn a knob; the backend knows no obstacle or cable limit, so the direction is the operator's responsibility; a measurement runs at a slew-rate preset, not at the guide rate; the simulator's slowest preset (0.5°/s) is too fast to show a polar error within a test, so the analysis of real divergence is tested in `core`; sessions do not survive a restart.

## Flat Wizard Contract

- `FlatWizardSessions` (`api/src/flatwizard.ts`) acquires FLAT frames for one camera, filter group after filter group, as one `camera.flatWizard` operation holding the camera, and the assigned filter wheel, flat panel, and dust cover when the request names them, from start to end, including while it waits for the operator. Each filter group searches its exposure with trial frames, freezes it, then takes final frames until its count of accepted frames was published. The light stays constant within a group: there is no automatic brightness search or sky flat, and the wizard builds no master itself: every published frame is registered in the Calibration Library, with the camera and the filter the file may leave out, and a registration that fails costs the session nothing. A session that asks for masters (`generateMasters`, the form's Generate masters after acquisition) hands the frames of every filter it completed to the library once it ended, however it ended (`flatSessionEnded`, after the registrations of its frames), which may queue an automatic master flat per filter (see Calibration Library Contract, Jobs); otherwise its flats wait in the library for a person, and a master that cannot start never touches the session. Results link to the library and offer **Prepare Matching Dark Flats**, which fills the form of the Dark Library Wizard with one group per exposure saved and moves nothing.
- Every exposure goes through the shared `CameraCapturer` with the camera settings of the request (`FlatWizardCaptureSettings`: gain, offset, binning, subframe, readout mode), frame type FLAT, FITS transfer, and the assigned wheel snooped into the header, written to a staging directory `.flatwizard-<sessionId>` inside `flats/` of the output directory (`directory`, or the default captures directory), where every accepted flat is saved, whatever its camera or filter. Frames are measured by `FlatWizardAnalyzer` (`api/src/flatwizard.analysis.ts`) on a worker of its own (`api/src/flatwizard.worker.ts`, embedded by `app/build.ts`), which reads the file in the digital numbers of its source (`sampleScale: 'digital'`), never through the normalized documents of the image store, and returns the toolkit's single-frame analysis without maps and, for a final frame, its temporal sample (`measureFlatSequenceFrame`) instead of the decoded image. Each frame carries the operating point the session commanded that a header may leave out, its readout mode and the origin of its subframe, and the analyzer adds the output bit depth of its file, so the frames of a session can be proven homogeneous.
- Each filter group names a 0-based filter `slot`. The filter is named by the name the wheel reports for that slot, or `Filter <slot + 1>` when it reports none or there is no wheel (`filterName`), resolved once when the session starts (`FlatWizardFilterRun.name`); that name goes into the file names, the frame context, and every message. The filter is selected by moving the wheel to the slot (confirmed by the wheel's reported position), or, without a wheel, by the operator, who puts the filter of that slot in place. The light comes from the panel at the group's `brightness` (driver units; absent keeps the reported one) and switched on, both confirmed by the panel's reported state, then `settleTime` seconds when something changed; without a panel the operator readies the light. A manual step publishes `pending` with a fresh `stepId` (`waitingForFilter`, which also covers a manual light, or `waitingForIllumination`), and only `POST /api/flat-wizard/:sessionId/continue` with that `stepId` resumes it; a stale or repeated confirmation is refused with 409 `staleStep`, and nothing else (tab, tool, profile, reconnection) confirms a step.
- Levels are observed DN with an unknown pedestal. The plane driving the exposure is the brightest plane of the first usable trial; only it is held to the group's `targetRange`, while every plane is checked for clipping and non-finite samples. A trial is `usable`, `clipped` (no observation; the exposure halves, and a clip at the minimum exposure fails), or `noSignal` (fails). An out-of-target first observation is followed by a second distinct exposure (twice or half, within the bounds), never a proportional guess; then `estimateFlatExposure` interpolates or fits an affine response. Every exposure the search derives is rounded to whole microseconds, the unit the Calibration Library compares exposures in, so a group, its file names, and its dark-flats share one exact exposure. Levels above the target that it cannot explain are taken as saturation, since a sensor can top out below the storage range where no sample counts as clipped: the longer observations are dropped and the exposure halves, and at the minimum exposure the search fails. The search fails with an explanation when the target needs an exposure outside the bounds, the estimate is invalid, it would repeat an exposure, or `maximumTrials` is exceeded. Trial files are deleted after measurement and never published.
- A final frame is `accepted` when its target and finite-sample checks pass and clipping is not present, even when its absence cannot be proven without `effectiveClip` (the frame then carries `effectiveClipUnknown`); `rejected` when a check fails; `inconclusive` otherwise. An accepted frame is published under `{date}_{time}_{camera}_FLAT_{filter}_{seq}.fit` by a hard link of its original bytes (an exclusive copy where links are unsupported), never replacing an existing file; a rejected or inconclusive one is deleted. More than `maximumRejections` rejected or inconclusive frames, or a failed publication, fail the session; published frames always stay.
- Once a group has its frames, `finalizeFlatSequence` evaluates their temporal stability from the retained samples (outliers at 5 robust sigmas); fewer than three frames, or frames the toolkit refuses as one stack, are `notEvaluated`. The result only describes the acquisition: no frame is deleted, replaced, or modified for it.
- An assigned dust cover (`cover`, refused with 422 when it cannot open and close) is closed before the first frame (`closingCover`, confirmed by its reported state) and held for the whole session. A cover already closed when the session starts stays closed afterwards; one the session closed, or began to close, is opened again once the session ended, however it ended, after the light was switched off.
- A light the session switched on is switched off after the operation ended, with the panel taken again if it is free; otherwise, and likewise for a cover that cannot be opened again, `cleanupWarning` says what was left. Stopping (`POST /api/flat-wizard/:sessionId/stop`, 409 `notRunning` once ended) cancels the operation at once: the exposure in flight is aborted and lost, a measurement or a manual step is abandoned, and the request resolves after the devices were released and the light cleanup ran. A disconnection of a held device cancels the session the same way.
- Session states: `preparing`, `closingCover`, `waitingForFilter`, `movingFilter`, `waitingForIllumination`, `settingLight`, `settling`, `exposing`, `measuring`, `saving`, `assessingSequence`, `stopping`, then `completed`, `cancelled`, or `failed`; filter groups go `pending`, `calibrating`, `acquiring`, then `completed`, `failed`, or `cancelled`, with their counts of trials, accepted, rejected, inconclusive, and failed publications.
- `POST /api/connections/:id/cameras/:deviceId/flat-wizard` starts a session (202 with `sessionId` and `operationId`; a repeated token answers the same session), refused with 404 for an unknown camera, wheel, panel, or cover and 409 when one is disconnected or held, or the camera's session is running. Sessions live in memory, the latest of each camera: `GET /api/flat-wizard` and `GET /api/flat-wizard/:sessionId` are the snapshot and the complete record with the latest 200 exposures, and `flatWizard:updated` summaries carry the latest exposure and, while a frame is exposed, its progress as the driver reports it (`exposure`: elapsed and remaining microseconds and percent, cleared once the frame was read). File names are published without their directory.
- Every frame is also decoded from its staging file into the image store and recorded in the capture frame registry (origin `flatWizard`, the `frameId` of its exposure in the session; see Capture Frames Contract), so the camera tab shows it; an accepted frame becomes saved under its published file, while trial and rejected frames stay previewed only. A frame that cannot be decoded is reported as `previewError` and never fails the session.
- Limitations: without a wheel, the filter name only names the files and the session; it is not written into the FITS header; sessions do not survive a restart.

## Dark Library Wizard Contract

- `DarkLibraryWizardSessions` (`api/src/darklibrarywizard.ts`) acquires the raw DARK frames of a capture plan for one camera, group after group, until each group has its accepted frames, as one `camera.darkLibraryWizard` operation holding the camera and only the wheel, panel, and cover its protection selected, from start to end, including while it is paused or waits for the operator. It is a camera acquisition: browsing, importing, masters, bias, reuse by the Sequencer, and defect maps are not part of it, and no pixel it saves is ever calibrated, debayered, or rewritten. A plan records what its frames are for (`purpose: 'darkFlat'`, absent for a dark), since the same light-blocked capture serves both and a driver may write either as DARK, and whether it wants masters (`generateMasters`), both optional fields of the request. Every accepted frame is handed to the Calibration Library as soon as it is published (`registerDark`), with what its plan declares about it, and the plan once its session ended, however it ended (`darkSessionEnded`, after the registrations of its frames), which, when the plan asked for masters, may queue one master per complete group (see Calibration Library Contract, Jobs); a master that fails never touches the session. A group short of its frames stays a state of the library: a provisional master is an operator's decision there.
- A plan is immutable: its groups (exposure in seconds, target temperature in °C or none, accepted frames), the camera settings shared by them (`DarkPlanCapture`: gain, offset, binning, subframe, readout mode), the protection, the thermal protocol, and the rejections allowed per group. A group is identified by the exact values of its settings (`darkGroupKey`), never by a rounded label, and groups run so one temperature is finished before the next, keeping the requested order within it (`orderDarkGroups`). The physical camera is identified by `hardwareId`, never by a connection id, a role, or a name. Limits of size live in the form (`DARK_LIBRARY_WIZARD_MAXIMUM_*`); the server checks only facts about devices and plans, and the fixed frame type is DARK, the transfer FITS, uncompressed.
- Protection is any combination of a wheel slot (`darkFilter`, 0-based), a closed cover, an unlit panel, and a manual cap. Each selected device must be present: one that is missing refuses the start with `protectionUnavailable` instead of becoming manual, and a slot the wheel does not have is refused with `invalidFilterSlot`, because the commander clamps a slot it cannot reach. The order is fixed so a cover never moves with a lit panel: `disablingPanel`, `movingDarkFilter`, `closingCover`, each confirmed by the reported state. What hardware cannot verify, the manual cap, the opacity of the filter at the slot (its name proves nothing), and an unlit panel when it is the only barrier, is the operator's to ensure before starting: the start, and a resume, are that assertion, and no step asks for it. Only `POST /api/dark-library-wizard/:sessionId/continue` with the published `stepId` confirms a step; a stale or repeated one is refused with 409 `staleStep`.
- The verified state is read again synchronously right before every exposure, through the capturer's `beforeExposure` hook, with nothing awaited until the command leaves. A device that left it publishes a `protectionLost` step and no frame is taken until it returned.
- Temperature is what the sensor reports. A group with a target needs `canSetTemperature` and a thermometer, or the start is refused with `temperatureUnavailable`. The cooler is switched on if it can be, the target is commanded once, and the session waits (`cooling`, `stabilizing`) until readings stay inside the tolerance for the hold time, failing with `temperatureTimeout` otherwise. The defaults are ±0.5 °C, 120 s, and 30 min. A reading stands until the driver changes it, since drivers and the thermometer manager report only changes: silence is recorded as the `age` of the reading and fails nothing, and only a recent Alert does. A reading out of tolerance before an exposure repeats the hold without commanding again. During an exposure readings are sampled every second into a trace (start, end, extremes, seconds out of tolerance); more than 10 % of the exposure out of tolerance rejects the frame (`temperatureExcursion`), less only notes it (`temperatureTransient`, accepted with warnings). A group without a target records what the camera reports and claims nothing, and a camera without a thermometer records `temperatureUnverified`. The cooler is never switched off, warmed, or given back a setpoint.
- Every exposure goes through the shared `CameraCapturer`, one frame per capture, into the staging directory of its plan, and is measured by `DarkLibraryWizardAnalyzer` (`api/src/darklibrarywizard.analysis.ts`) on a worker of its own (`api/src/darklibrarywizard.worker.ts`, embedded by `app/build.ts`) in the digital numbers of the file, reusing the statistics of the toolkit's flat analysis without its targets or verdict. `assessDarkFrame` decides it. A proven failure rejects it: unreadable, a header that reports another frame type, exposure, gain, offset, or binning, a size off the request by more than 16 pixels (drivers trim) or other than the first frame of its group, non-finite samples. A keyword the driver omits is only a note. A suspicion of light never rejects a frame: 1 % of a plane at the clipping limit, a level or tile spread above the baseline of the group once 3 frames were accepted, or a level above `m(t₀)·t/t₀` of a shorter exposure at the same operating point, which holds whatever the pedestal is. Amp glow, hot pixels, gradients, and pedestal are the signal a dark records, so no rule uses an absolute brightness or the gradient of one frame.
- Verdicts: `accepted` and `acceptedWithWarnings` count, `rejected` (a proven failure) and `incomplete` (never examined) do not. A suspicion on the first frame of a group, when `verifyFirstFrame` is on, and two in a row anywhere, wait in `waitingForDecision` with a `suspectedLight` step: continuing records the frame as acknowledged, and stopping quarantines it. Other suspicions are recorded without stopping. More than `maximumRejections` rejections fail the session with `verificationFailed`. A group completes on accepted frames, never on commanded exposures, and `GET /api/dark-library-wizard/:sessionId` keeps the latest 200 exposures.
- A plan lives in the memory of its session (`DarkCapturePlan` in `api/src/darklibrarywizard.plan.ts`), and nothing of it is written but its frames: they live together in `<directory>/darks/`, whatever the camera, plan, or group, each named by when it was taken, its camera, its role with the exposure and target temperature of its group (or, without a target, the sensor temperature measured during the frame, rounded to whole degrees), and its sequence (`2026-11-03_21-04-05-123_ASI294_DARK_300s_-10C_0001.fit`, `DARKFLAT` for dark-flats); the rejected ones in `darks/rejected/`, and the frames being acquired in `darks/.staging/<planId>/`, a directory per session that goes once the session ends with it empty. An accepted frame is published without replacing anything (`publishFrame`), and only then counts and is registered in the Calibration Library. A frame number is unique in its group. Every start makes a new plan, and nothing lists, completes, or reuses one: what was acquired is what the Calibration Library registered. Nothing is kept to resume: a plan that ended short of its frames stays that way, a session the server did not finish ends with it and what its staging held stays there uncounted, and the missing frames are a new start. Nothing is ever deleted, and nothing starts a device by itself.
- Pausing (`/pause`) takes effect after the current frame was examined and saved, and keeps every device held, reported as the operation's progress, because releasing them would let the protection be undone unnoticed; a pause can be withdrawn until then. `/resume` is the operator's assertion of the protection; the verified devices are read again before the next exposure and the temperature is confirmed again. Stopping (`/stop`) cancels the operation at once, the exposure in flight is lost, and published frames stay. A disconnection of a held device cancels the session the same way.
- Once the operation ended (`restoring`), whatever staging holds is quarantined, the panel is confirmed off whatever it was before, the wheel returns to the slot it started at if the session moved it, and the cover opens if the session closed it and the panel is known to be off. A cover found closed stays closed. A cancelled operation cannot start new work, so each device is taken again only if no other operation took it first; what could not be restored is listed in `cleanupWarnings` and recorded in the plan.
- Session states: `preparing`, `disablingPanel`, `movingDarkFilter`, `closingCover`, `waitingForProtection` (a `protectionLost` step), `cooling`, `stabilizing`, `exposing`, `measuring`, `publishing`, `waitingForDecision`, `paused`, `stopping`, `restoring`, then `completed`, `cancelled`, or `failed`; groups go `pending`, `acquiring`, then `completed`, `failed`, or `cancelled`.
- `POST /api/connections/:id/cameras/:deviceId/dark-library-wizard` starts a session (202 with `sessionId`, `operationId`, and `planId`; a repeated token answers the same session). Refusals and a failed session carry a `DarkLibraryWizardErrorCode`. Sessions live in memory, the latest of each camera: `GET /api/dark-library-wizard` and `GET /api/dark-library-wizard/:sessionId` are the snapshot and the complete record, and `darkLibraryWizard:updated` carries every change, without paths. No route lists or reads plans.
- Every examined frame is decoded from staging into the image store and recorded as the latest frame of its camera (origin `darkLibraryWizard`); one that cannot be decoded is reported as `previewError` and is still examined from its file.
- Limitations: an image cannot prove the absence of light, and in the first group of a plan there is no baseline, so only saturation raises a suspicion there; a driver that silently stops reporting temperature looks like a stable sensor, and only the age of the reading tells them apart; between the end of a session and its restoring, another operation can take a device, which is then reported rather than restored; the size checked for a full frame assumes the driver's reported maximum; a directory deep enough to pass the 260 characters of a Windows path fails the capture, which is reported and counts nothing; the provenance a driver leaves out of a header is kept only in the Calibration Library, since files are never rewritten.

## Calibration Library Contract

The Calibration Library is the single owner of which calibration frames exist, whether they can be combined, and which masters were made of them. It needs no connection, claims no device, and takes no part in `OperationRegistry`; a wizard only acquires frames, and asks for a master through the same path as a person. The library lists the masters that match a target (`POST /api/calibration-library/resolve-preview`, metadata only) and never picks one. Image Stacking calibrates its lights with the masters an operator chose among those and reads them through `pinnedMaster`, which answers a file only while its SHA-256 is the one chosen and it is neither unavailable nor stale, and `lease`, which refuses the deletion of a master (`masterInUse`) while an analysis reads it.

- **Sources.** Frames come from three places, always by reference: accepted frames the Dark Library Wizard publishes, registered as they are published with what their plan declares (`registerDark`; the library compares files itself, and a missing file can come back); frames the Flat Wizard publishes; and directories and files an operator imports. An import (`POST /imports` with `{ paths, recursive }`, refused with `404 notFound` when a path cannot be read) is a cancellable scan of headers of directories and single files, each file found once whichever path or name reached it, reviewed before it is committed, that skips `.staging`, `rejected`, and `.flatwizard-*`; it may copy files under the library instead. Frames are never written, and nothing is inferred from a file or directory name. Only an operator deletes: `DELETE /sources/:sourceId` removes one frame and its file, wherever it is, and `DELETE /groups/:groupId` every frame of a group with their files; masters made of them stay, a frame whose file cannot be deleted stays, and a frame or group a master is being built from cannot go.
- **Evidence.** Every value is `verified` (the file), `planDeclared` (an immutable plan or the wizard session that took it), `operatorDeclared` (an override an operator declared, only over an unknown value), or `unknown`. Nothing identifies a physical camera: frames of the same geometry and settings combine whatever camera took them, and the camera name a header or a wizard gives is only shown and filtered by. There is no optical train in the application, so a flat groups by `FILTER`, `TELESCOP`, and `FOCALLEN`, and what is missing asks for a review.
- **Compatibility.** `calibrationlibrary.compatibility.ts` is pure. A versioned key holds every property that must agree per kind; exposures are integer microseconds, so 179.9 s and 180 s never merge. Between frames, a known difference blocks, unknown never matches a known value, and a group has an explained state (`ready`, `needsReview`, `incomplete`, `incompatible`, `missingFiles`, `notSupported`), never a score. A master matches a target (`matchesTarget`) when every property both know agrees, a dark within 1 °C of the temperatures of the target (of their range when its frames vary, so the lights of a camera whose temperature drifts or oscillates take any dark inside the range they span) and a flat with its filter; what either does not know never refuses one, since the operator chooses among those listed. There is no closest master, no dark scaling, no temperature interpolation.
- **Products.** Every build publishes a new immutable master, however many there are of the same frames and recipe: a `master.fit` in `products/<masterId>/`, and its row, which says everything else about it (recipe, metadata, diagnostics, sources with their digests, dependencies). `masterId` is an incremental integer that is never used again (`AUTOINCREMENT`), and with the time it was published tells masters of one group apart; a master keeps the id of the group of its frames, which lists its latest masters. `DELETE /masters/:masterId` removes one master with its directory, refused (`masterInUse`) while a master or a job depends on it, which the database enforces too. A master is `unavailable` while its file is missing or of another size and `stale` while a master it depends on is unavailable, and returns to the state it was published with once neither holds; `GET /masters?status=` filters by it, and `?groupId=` by group. Dark, bias, and dark-flat masters are Float32 FITS in DN, keep their pedestal, and take no normalization. A master flat exists only from flats corrected by a master dark-flat or bias, is dimensionless, has a median of 1 per plane (CFA phase or channel), and is marked `calibratedNormalized`, so no calibrator subtracts a pedestal twice. A master in DN must be read with `sampleScale: 'digital'`: the normalized read rescales float data outside 0..1 per image, and `calibrationMasterImage` of the toolkit converts it with the `fullScaleDn` of its master. The header is written from metadata, never copied, so no stale `BZERO` reaches it.
- **Jobs.** Master builds and import scans are jobs of this process, kept in memory only, with the exact frames a build saw: they survive the tab, never the server, and nothing of them is written but the staging of a running build. The latest 100 ended jobs are kept, and older ones forgotten. A request always queues a new job (`202` with its `jobId`), and only a repeated token meets the job it started; a completed job names the master it published. One master integrates at a time on `calibrationlibrary.worker.ts`, with its own queue instead of `AnalysisScheduler`, which needs an open document. A wizard's session end queues automatic jobs, from the frames of one group each and only when every frame is usable and nothing but the temperature asks for a review: `flatSessionEnded` one master flat per completed filter, corrected by the newest matching master dark-flat, else the newest matching master bias (never a stale or unavailable one, and none is queued without one), and `darkSessionEnded`, when the plan asked for masters, one master per complete group. An automatic job waits, checked again every 15 s, while an operation of a `camera.` kind runs (a capture, a wizard, guiding). Progress is a heartbeat: there is no limit on a job, but a worker silent for two minutes is terminated. A restart forgets every job, the masters a wizard asked for among them, and removes all staging; nothing asks for them again. No route lists, cancels, or retries a master job (the snapshot carries the active ones and the latest of the wizards): a new request builds a master again, and the group shows its latest build; only the scan of an import can be cancelled (`/imports/:jobId/cancel`).
- **Memory.** A strip of rows per frame fits a budget of 256 MiB. Uncompressed FITS reads rows from the file (`FitsImageReader.readRows`). Tile-compressed FITS and XISF decode once into a raw spill file in `staging/`, one frame in memory at a time, which trades that memory for disk; `recipes/validate` reports it.
- **Publication.** The worker writes and reads back its file in `staging/<jobId>/`; the server compares the sources with what the job saw, takes the next master id and records the master as `publishing` in one transaction, renames the directory into `products/<masterId>/`, and only then makes it `published` with the digests of its sources. Deleting a master records it as `deleting` before its directory goes. At the next start, a `publishing` master whose `master.fit` has the recorded size and SHA-256 is published and any other is forgotten, its id staying taken; a `deleting` one loses its directory and row; a directory of `products/` without a row moves to `calibration-library/orphaned/<name>-<uuid>/` and is reported, never adopted nor deleted; and all staging is removed. A change of a source is seen by size and modification time at publication. Files reached through two names are told apart by their device and inode, read as a bigint so a 64-bit NTFS file id keeps every digit.
- **Persistence.** `CalibrationLibraryStore` keeps the frames (`calibration_sources`, with what an operator declared about them) and the masters (`calibration_masters`, `calibration_master_sources`, `calibration_master_dependencies`) in the operational database; the products stay files. Jobs and what an import found are never written. An override, a role, an exclusion, or a deletion changes its rows in one transaction, with no history.
- **Boundaries.** `GET /api/calibration-library` (`CalibrationLibrarySnapshot`: an epoch per process, a revision, frames and masters counted by state and role, the camera names, the active jobs, and the latest jobs of the wizards), `calibrationLibrary:changed`, `calibrationLibrary:jobUpdated`, and `calibrationLibrary:masterUpdated` carry ids and no paths, and job errors hold file names or codes, never a filesystem message; a path comes only from a detail route. Frames with a BITPIX of 64 are `notSupported`.
- **Calibrating a light.** Image Stacking and Live Stacking apply masters; any other calibrator reads `/api/calibration-library/masters`, keeps the `masterId` and `contentDigest` it used, reads the file with `sampleScale: 'digital'`, and converts it with `calibrationMasterImage(master, fullScaleDn)` (1 for a flat). The recipe is `(light - dark) / flat`, or `(light - bias) / flat`, on the raw mosaic before debayering: a master dark already holds the bias, so no bias is subtracted after it, and a master flat is passed to `calibrate` with `flatRepresentation: 'calibratedNormalized'`, which refuses a dark-flat because the flat already includes it. Its dead pixels are not repaired: `invalidFlat: 'passthrough'` leaves those samples without flat correction. A candidate is what `matchesTarget` accepts, never the closest one, and with `darkScaling: 'none'`.
- **Tool.** Tools → Calibration Library (`web/src/tools/calibrationlibrary/`, `/tools/calibration-library[/<group id>]`) mirrors the server through `web/src/calibrationlibrary/calibrationlibrary.ts`, a snapshot and the jobs in `SERVER_STORES` and pages fetched by the view, again whenever a change arrives. Library shows the coverage of a page of groups by camera name and operating point (a group covered only by a master of its own role), their states, and the latest master of each, a flat group whose frames have several exposures named by their range (`exposures` of its summary), since only a bias corrects them together; Frames shows each frame, how each value is known, and the review that declares an unknown value or leaves a frame out, without asking why; an import declares the role of a file its header does not say the same way; Frames and Masters open a file in Images through `openImage`; Masters lists every master, named by its id and the time it was published (`masterName`), with its provenance, and the details of a master list under its state what puts it there (`masterWarnings`: too few frames, what was accepted when building it, a missing file, an unavailable correction). The camera filter lists the camera names of the frames. Import Frames opens a dialog that adds directories, and files several at a time (the `multiple` file chooser), both choosers starting in the directory listed last (`lastCalibrationDirectory`, view state in localStorage), lists them to remove one, and then scans and reviews them; committing adds the frames and closes the dialog. A group that needs a review says that what is listed cannot be verified from the files, and that accepting it in Build Master, or declaring the missing values on its frames, is how a master gets built. A frame, a group, and a master are deleted after a confirmation. Build Master builds a new master on every request, of a dark group as a Dark or a Dark-Flat (`MasterRequest.kind`, for dark-flats a capture recorded as darks; the master keeps its group, and frames of any other role keep theirs), stays busy, with its progress, while the latest build of its group runs, and shows why it failed. The build form asks the server to explain every change (`recipes/validate`) and lists only what the recipe adds to the findings of its group, so nothing about compatibility is decided in the tab. Viewers read a master through the normalized pipeline, which is for looking at it, not for measuring.

## Image Stacking Contract

Image Stacking turns light frames saved on the server into per-filter integrations and color composites. It needs no connection, claims no device, and takes no part in `OperationRegistry`. It covers the project model (importing, grouping, and reviewing frames), the planning of a stack (calibration decisions, the measurement and registration of every frame, and validation), its integration into one linear master per filter, and the color composition of those masters. Drizzle is not provided, and masters and composites carry no WCS.

- **Projects.** A project (`stack_projects`) is one intended target or field: a name an operator gives it (`POST /projects`) and edits from its revision (`PATCH /projects/:projectId`, `409 conflict` otherwise), and the light frames chosen for it. Deleting a project (`DELETE /projects/:projectId`) deletes its rows, composites, and products, never a frame file.
- **Imports.** `POST /projects/:projectId/imports` with `{ paths, recursive }` starts a cancellable scan of headers of directories and single files (`scanDirectory` and `inspectFile` of the Calibration Library, bounded depth, count, and concurrency), never pixels; nothing is written, moved, or copied. A path inside the directories of Nebulosa (`image-stacking/` and `calibration-library/` under the data directory) is refused (`400 managedPath`), and a candidate inside one cannot be added. A file reached through two names, in one scan or across imports, is one frame (its device:inode identity, `UNIQUE (project_id, identity)`), and a known one is not added twice. A completed import reports how many lights and unclassified files a commit would add, and its review lists the candidates by page with their role, filter, target, and exposure; `/imports/:jobId/commit` adds lights, and files whose header says no role either declared lights or kept unclassified: every usable candidate, only those it names (`paths`), or all but those it skips (`skipped`). Calibration frames, masters, unreadable headers, and BITPIX 64 never enter. Import jobs live in memory (the latest 50 ended ones), survive the tab, and end with the process.
- **Evidence.** Roles and filters come from headers (`IMAGETYP`/`FRAME` through `readFitsProvenance`, `FILTER`), never from a file or directory name. An unclassified frame joins no group until an operator declares it a light; a light whose header names no filter, or a blank one, belongs to None (`NO_FILTER`) and is stacked like any other filter, unless an operator declares its filter (`operatorDeclared`, refused over a filter the header names, `409 verifiedMetadata`). Declarations are recorded among the warnings of the frame, and no header is ever rewritten. `OBJECT` is a hint: more than one target in a project is reported, never resolved; overlap is left to registration.
- **Filter groups.** A group is the lights of one filter: exactly the name their headers write, only trimmed (`filterKeyOf`), never classified, normalized, or merged, so names spelled differently (Ha, H-alpha, ha) are different filters; groups are computed, not stored, and sorted by filter. A group reports its frames, the usable ones (included with an available file), their exposure, and the number of distinct acquisition settings among them (`cohortKeyOf`: camera name, geometry, BITPIX, CFA, binning, gain, offset, readout mode, and exposure; unknown values never agree with known ones).
- **Files.** A frame is `available`, `missing`, or `changed` by the size and modification time of its file, checked when the service opens and on `POST /refresh` or `/projects/:projectId/refresh`; `corrupt` stays. An operator leaves a frame out or includes it again (`PATCH /frames/:sourceId`), and `DELETE /frames/:sourceId` takes it out of the project, never deleting its file.
- **Cohorts and calibration.** A cohort is the usable lights of one filter group that share their acquisition settings (`cohortIdOf`: group key and `cohortKeyOf`); cohorts are computed, and only the masters an operator chose are stored (`stack_cohorts`), so a choice returns with its frames. `GET /projects/:projectId/cohorts` matches each cohort against the Calibration Library (`resolvePreview` with its geometry and settings, its exposure, the median and the range of its sensor temperatures, and its most frequent filter name) and offers every matching dark, flat, and bias, newest first. `PATCH /projects/:projectId/cohorts/:cohortId` replaces the choice with `{ calibration: { dark?, flat?, bias? } }`, each `{ masterId, digest }` one of those offered (`409 incompatibleMaster` otherwise); a bias is dropped with a dark, which holds the pedestal, and a cohort with none chosen is not calibrated. Nothing asks for a camera, a reason, or a decision.
- **Analysis.** `POST /projects/:projectId/analyses` (202) refuses while another analysis or a run works (`409 jobConflict`), or while a chosen master is no longer available (`409 masterUnavailable`), and leases the chosen masters until it ends; a cohort with none chosen is measured uncalibrated. It measures every usable light whose measurement is missing or stale on a worker of its own (`imagestacking.worker.ts`, embedded by `app/build.ts`), one frame at a time: SHA-256, a digital read, `calibrate` with the masters of its cohort (`darkScaling: 'none'`, `flatRepresentation: 'calibratedNormalized'`, `invalidFlat: 'passthrough'`; a bias only without a dark), `detectStars` from SNR 5, and `measureSubframeQuality`. Each measurement is saved as it arrives with its fingerprint (measurement version, file size and time, chosen masters), so a cancelled or failed analysis keeps what it measured and the next one resumes. Registration then runs on the same worker, since matching costs tens of milliseconds per frame (about a minute for a thousand frames), from the 100 brightest stars of each frame, which the server reads back: every frame onto the reference of its group (the pinned frame, or the best by `referenceQualityScore`) and every group reference onto the project reference (that of the pinned group, or the group reference with the most stars); a group that does not register fails alone. The transform of a frame onto the project grid is the composition of both, so a stack resamples it once. A worker silent for two minutes is terminated; `POST /analyses/:jobId/cancel` stops one between frames.
- **Validation.** `GET /projects/:projectId/validation` reads no pixel and lists findings: blockers (`noLights`, `flatWithoutPedestal` of a cohort with a flat but neither a dark nor a bias, whose pedestal divided by the flat would imprint its dust and vignetting inverted, `masterUnavailable`, `masterIncompatible`, `analysisStale` when a frame, a chosen master, a reference, or the frames of a group changed since, `registrationFailed` of a group), reviews (`multipleTargets`), and warnings (`partialCalibration` of a cohort without a flat, and of one without a dark or a bias when it has no flat either, `multipleSetups`, and frames that stay out of the stack: `measurementFailed`, `registrationFailed` of a frame, `belowThresholds` of the quality thresholds in `PUT /projects/:projectId/settings`), with an estimate of the memory of a stack (26 bytes per sample of the largest frame). Settings also hold the reference pins; a frame failing a threshold stays in the project.
- **Runs.** `POST /projects/:projectId/preflight` answers what a run would do with a recipe (`StackingRecipe`: combination, weighting, normalization, interpolation, minimum coverage, sigma limits) and a memory budget (2 GiB by default): per filter group, its frames, its exposure, a master it would reuse, and its plan (`integrationPlan`: in memory for sum and the averages, otherwise spilled to staging at 5 bytes per frame and pixel of every channel plus one, and combined in strips of rows that fit the budget), with what stops it: a blocker of validation (`409 notReady`), a group that does not fit the budget (`insufficientMemory`), a spill larger than the free space of the staging disk (`insufficientSpace`), or a chosen master no longer available (`masterUnavailable`). `POST /projects/:projectId/runs` (202) with a `token` starts one and answers its integration job; a run is a job of the process only, never stored (only the masters it publishes are), so the same token answers the same job while the process keeps it, and a failed run names in its job error each filter group that failed and why. No run or analysis starts while another works (`409 jobConflict`). A group integrates its usable lights whose measurement is current, that registered, and that pass the thresholds, the reference of the group first. On the worker each frame is read in digital numbers, calibrated as it was measured, debayered when it is a color mosaic, and resampled once onto the project grid (the size of the project reference) through the composition of its transform onto its group reference with that of the group onto the project; pixels of an unusable flat are masked (`invalidFlatMask`). It is matched to the warped group reference (`solveGlobalNormalizationPlanes`, per channel) on a nearest-neighbour resampling of both, which keeps their samples as they are: the interpolation of the recipe smooths noise by an amount that depends on the subpixel phase of each frame, which a dispersion estimator would read as a gain. The frame is then resampled with the interpolation of the recipe, weighted (`frameWeight`), and combined (`accumulateSamples` or `combineStrip`); a pixel covered by fewer frames than the minimum coverage is NaN. Masters are never cropped, so every master of a project shares its grid. A run is cancelled through `POST /runs/:runId/cancel`, and a worker silent for two minutes is terminated.
- **Products.** Each group is published as soon as it is staged, so a cancelled or failed run keeps what it published: its frames and chosen masters are compared again with what the run planned, its row is written as publishing (`stack_products`, with its frames in `stack_product_sources`), its directory renamed into `products/<productId>/` (`master.fit`, Float32 with a header written from metadata, and `coverage.fit`, the frames that covered each pixel), and only then published. A group whose inputs (`INTEGRATION_VERSION`, frames with their SHA-256, weights, and transforms, chosen masters, grid, and recipe) match a published master that is still ready reuses it. A product is `stale` while a frame it integrated left the project, is not available, or was measured with other content, or while a chosen master is no longer available, and `unavailable` while a file is missing or of another size; it is never rewritten. `GET /projects/:projectId/products` lists them, `GET /products/:productId` adds the paths of its files (to open them in Images) and its frames, and `DELETE /products/:productId` removes one, refused while a composite reads it (`409 productInUse`, also enforced by `stack_composite_sources`). On start, a product left publishing becomes published when its master has the recorded size and SHA-256 and is forgotten otherwise, one left deleting loses its directory and row, a directory of `products/` without a row moves to `orphaned/`, and staging is emptied.
- **Composition.** A composition (`StackingComposition`) mixes planes of masters of one project, never light frames, into linear RGB: each source is `{ productId, plane, gain, offset }`, each output channel the weighted mean of its sources (`mixChannelRows`), and an optional luminance source replaces the luminance of the mix by a weight, matched by background and scale first and keeping channel ratios (`blendLuminanceRows`). `POST /projects/:projectId/compositions/validate` reports blockers (a source that is not a master of the project or has no such plane, masters on different grids or unavailable, an empty channel, a weight, gain, or offset out of range, a luminance out of range) and warnings (a stale master), with the fingerprint of the inputs (`COMPOSITION_VERSION`, the composition, the SHA-256 of each master) and a published composite it would reuse. `POST /projects/:projectId/compositions/preview` answers a JPEG at most 768 pixels on its longest side of every k-th row and column, mixed by the same functions and stretched by one linked automatic curve for display; only the latest preview is computed, and an earlier one answers `409 superseded`. `POST /projects/:projectId/compositions` (202) composes on the worker, one composition at a time: a first pass over strips of rows finds the box of the pixels every mixed master covers and samples the luminance match, a second writes the cropped channels, joined into one Float32 `composite.fit` in `composites/<compositeId>/` (`stack_composites`, published like a product, with its origin on the project grid). Its masters cannot be deleted while it or a preview reads them. A composite is `stale` while a master it mixes is not ready; `GET /projects/:projectId/composites`, `GET /composites/:compositeId` (with its path), `DELETE /composites/:compositeId`, and `POST /compositions/:jobId/cancel` complete it.
- **Boundaries.** `GET /api/image-stacking` (`ImageStackingSnapshot`), `imageStacking:changed`, and `imageStacking:jobUpdated` carry an epoch per process and a revision per change, and no paths; the progress of a long step (rows of a strip, frames registered) is published at most four times a second, and its last step always; lists show file names, and a path comes only from `GET /frames/:sourceId` (to open it in Images) and from the candidates of an import, which the operator chose.
- **Tool.** Tools → Image Stacking (`web/src/tools/imagestacking/`, `/tools/image-stacking[/<project id>]`) keeps only Import Frames in its header and Check Files by the project, and its sections scroll on their own on a phone. It mirrors the server through `projects.ts` in `SERVER_STORES`, and fetches the project, its groups, and a page of frames again whenever a change arrives. Filters lists the groups; Frames filters by group, role, file state, inclusion, and name, and leaves a frame out, includes it again, or removes it from the project (after a confirmation, never deleting its file) from its row; the panel shows how each value of a frame is known, opens it in Images, and declares a role or a filter the header does not say. An import chooses directories, and files several at a time (the `multiple` file chooser), both choosers starting in the directory listed last (`lastStackingDirectory`, view state in localStorage), reads their headers, and adds nothing until its review is committed: every usable candidate starts checked, a check, Select All, and Deselect All hold across pages (all but the unchecked, or only the checked), and committing adds the checked ones and closes the dialog. Calibration shows one card per cohort with a list of the matching masters of each kind, named by id, date, and frames, saved as soon as one is chosen (the bias list is off while a dark is chosen), and a flat chosen without a dark or a bias is flagged as needing one; Quality starts and cancels the analysis, edits the project reference and the thresholds (numeric fields, 0 for none), lists the measurements and transforms of the frames of one group with their reference pin, fetched again as the running analysis measures each frame, and shows the reviews and notes of the validation with only its blockers that keep an analysis from starting (`noLights` of the project and `masterUnavailable`, `blocksAnalysis`), which alone disable Analyze; the blockers of a stack are shown by Integration. Integration edits the recipe and the memory budget, shows the preflight of each filter, starts a run with a new token, and follows and cancels it; Results lists the masters with their state, opens a master or its coverage in Images, and deletes one; Color resolves a preset (RGB, LRGB, SHO, HOO, LSHO, `presets.ts`) to the newest usable mono master of each filter or names what is missing, edits the gains, the weights of each channel, and the luminance (Custom), previews the composition as the server renders it, and composes and lists the composites. The project, its cohorts, its validation, its products, and its composites are fetched again on every change.

## Live Stacking Contract

Live Stacking integrates the light frames of Camera Acquisition as they arrive, into online stacks a camera tab shows and an operator saves.
