It is a server-owned analysis session, not an operation: it never commands a device, holds none in `OperationRegistry`, and never stops or slows Camera Acquisition beyond the gate below. It covers the session of a camera (`api/src/livestacking.ts`), the grouping policy (`livestacking.groups.ts`), the engine of fields and stacks (`livestacking.engine.ts`, on `livestacking.worker.ts`), and the saved stacks (`livestacking.store.ts`).

- **Session.** One session per exact camera (connection id + device id), never a role, profile, or tab, started with `POST /api/connections/:id/cameras/:deviceId/live-stacking` (`LiveStackingStartRequest`: a token, the recipe, the calibration): `201` when started, `200` with the session its token already started, `409 sessionConflict` while another session of the camera runs; a stopped or failed one is closed first. It spans any number of captures and changes of filter, settings, or output mode, and outlives tabs and sockets. Its state is `waiting` or `processing` while it admits frames, `paused` (arrivals skipped, admitted frames still integrated), `stopping` (no arrival admitted, the admitted ones integrated), `stopped` (its stacks viewable and saveable until `DELETE /sessions/:sessionId` closes it), or `failed` (its worker failed and its stacks are lost, so a view or a save answers `409 workerFailed`). `GET /sessions/:sessionId` reads a session, and `POST /sessions/:sessionId/<command>` sends `pause` (of a running session), `resume` (of a paused one), `stop`, `abort`, or `reset` (not of a failed one), a command out of its state answering `409 invalidState`: `abort` skips the queued frames as aborted, completes the one being integrated, and keeps the stacks; `reset` discards the stacks and fields and keeps integrating the admitted frames. A restart forgets every session.
- **Frames.** The capture service feeds every LIGHT frame of Camera Acquisition to the session of its camera through its frame sink (see Capture Frames Contract), deduplicated by capture session and generation, never by name, time, or document. Preview Only frames are copied once and queued in memory; Preview + Save frames queue only their identity until their write succeeded, then are read from their file, never from a partial one, and fail to be delivered when the write fails or their capture ends first. Every received frame ends in exactly one of `accepted` (into one stack), `rejected` (read and refused: too few stars, poor quality, no matching or confirmed field, too little overlap, a failed normalization, an incompatible grid, no memory left, chosen masters unavailable, incompatible, or unreadable, an unreadable frame), `skipped` (on purpose: paused, aborted, not a light, an unclassified filter, unknown metadata, no masters chosen), or `deliveryFailed` (its write failed, its pixels or file never came, the backlog limit, the worker failed, the session failed); `received = queued + processing + held + accepted + rejected + skipped + deliveryFailed` always. Frames are only counted: why each one ended is not kept or served.
- **Backpressure.** At most `MAX_PENDING_PAYLOADS` (2) Preview Only frames of a camera are held in memory, the one being integrated included: while the queue is full, the `beforeFrame` gate of the capture waits before the next exposure is prepared (never during one), and a stop, an abort, a pause, a failure, or the end of either side releases it. Saved frames queue at most `MAX_SAVED_BACKLOG` (1000) identities; beyond either limit a frame fails to be delivered. Frames are integrated one at a time, in order, on a worker per session (`WorkerProcessor`), which is terminated when an integration or an export stalls past `INTEGRATION_STALL_TIMEOUT` (120 s) or crashes: the session fails, its pending frames fail to be delivered, and the capture goes on. Measured on a 4-core Intel Core i5-6500T, a 16-bit frame calibrated with a dark and a flat integrates in 0.45 s at 1 MP mono, 10 s at 26 MP mono, and 5.5 s as an 11.7 MP mosaic (star detection is 65 to 85% of it), and a 26 MP mono export takes 5.6 s: the queue of two keeps the camera busy whenever a frame integrates faster than the next one is exposed, the saved backlog is at most 2.8 hours of integration at 26 MP, and the stall timeout leaves ten times the slowest request.
- **Grouping.** The header of each frame decides its group (`inspectHeader` of the Calibration Library): the filter is exactly its `FILTER`, trimmed, as Image Stacking keeps it (None without a filter wheel snooped into the capture; unclassified, and skipped, when one was snooped but the header names none); a frame that is not a light, or whose size or exposure is unknown, is skipped. A segment is the filter, the acquisition cohort (`cohortKeyOf`: camera, geometry, sample type, CFA layout, binning, gain, offset, readout mode, exposure in integer microseconds, unknowns never matching known values) and the calibration policy (`segmentKeyOf`); temperature is not keyed. A field is one pointing and its grid: its reference is the first frame of its geometry (`geometryKeyOf`) with at least the minimum stars, and every stack of the field is resampled onto it (`LiveStackingField` of the toolkit), so stacks of different filters on one field can be composed. A frame joins the stack of its segment on the field it registers onto, the most recently used first; a frame that registers onto none is held, and a new field starts only once `FIELD_CONFIRMATION_FRAMES` (3) held frames register onto one another, or when an operator asks for one (`POST /sessions/:sessionId/fields`); otherwise the held frames are rejected (`unconfirmedField`, `fieldMismatch`). A field is never replaced or moved.
- **Integration.** Each stack is a toolkit `LiveStacker` on its field, whose photometric reference is its own first frame, so a filter is never normalized to another; its recipe (`LiveStackingRecipe`: `average` by default or `weighted-average` with a frame weighting, `background-scale` normalization, `bilinear` resampling, the minimum stars and overlap, optional HFD and SNR limits) is locked into the stacks of the session, and its sums are Float64. A rejected frame changes no stack. Quality only gates through the stars and the registration by default, so a dim narrowband frame is kept.
- **Calibration.** The masters an operator chose for a filter (`LiveStackingCalibration.filters`, by exact filter name) are pinned by id and content: for each frame, `pinnedMaster` must still return them and `matchesTarget` must accept them for that frame (a dark within 1 °C of its temperature and of its exact exposure), else the frame is rejected; they are leased while it is read and applied as Image Stacking does (`calibrateLight`: `(light - dark) / flat`, a bias only without a dark, the unusable pixels of a flat masked). The Calibration Library never chooses: a filter without masters is integrated uncalibrated, in stacks of its own, or skipped (`otherwise`). Changing the masters (`PUT /sessions/:sessionId/calibration`) starts other stacks for the frames that follow; no stack is ever recalibrated or mixes calibrated and uncalibrated frames.
- **Memory.** The engine reports the bytes it retains (field references, stacks by `liveStackerMemoryBytes`, held frames, the masters read), and every new field, stack, or held frame is refused with `capacityExceeded` when every session together, with their queued frames, would exceed `DEFAULT_LIVE_STACKING_MEMORY_BUDGET` (4 GiB); nothing is ever evicted. The retained bytes match `liveStackerMemoryBytes` with the reference and masters exactly: a 26 MP mono field retains 1170 MiB for its first stack with a dark and a flat and 846 MiB for each other filter, so the budget holds four 26 MP mono filters of one field, or one 26 MP color stack of a one-shot-color camera with the candidates of a new field. It bounds retained memory only: the transient work of a 26 MP frame, snapshot, or composition peaks 1.0 to 1.4 GiB above it, and the snapshot documents of the image store are not counted.
- **Views.** `POST /sessions/:sessionId/views` with a stack (`{ cohortId }`) or a composition (`{ composition }`: mono stacks of one field mixed as Image Stacking composes masters, `mixChannelRows` and `blendLuminanceRows`) answers a snapshot as a derived document of the image store (provenance `liveStacking`), with the header of a saved stack (the camera and settings of its first frame, `NCOMBINE`, `TOTALEXP`, and the method and masters in `HISTORY`; pixels no frame covered are 0 instead of NaN), so a save from the viewer toolbar writes what it integrates, taken again only when its stacks changed and the previous one is older than `SNAPSHOT_INTERVAL` (1.5 s); a session keeps at most four such documents open. A snapshot runs on the worker between integrations: a mono stack takes 20 ms at 1 MP and 0.45 s at 26 MP, a composition of two stacks with a luminance 0.13 s and 3.2 s, so a color view of frames slower than the interval takes about a third of the worker. Snapshots are linear: display stretches never change a stack or a save.
- **Saving.** `POST /sessions/:sessionId/exports` with a stack or a composition writes one consistent revision as a linear Float32 FITS (`stack.fit`, NaN where no frame covered a pixel; a stack also `coverage.fit`) whose header records the accepted frames, total exposure, method, and masters, in `live-stacking/staging/<id>/`, records it as publishing in `live_stack_products`, renames it into `live-stacking/products/<id>/`, and marks it published; Preview Only sessions save the same way and keep no light frame. `GET /api/live-stacking/products[/:productId]` lists them and gives the paths to open in Images; `DELETE` removes one. At start, interrupted publications are finished or forgotten, directories without a row are moved to `live-stacking/orphaned/`, and staging is cleared.
- **Boundaries.** `GET /api/live-stacking` (`LiveStackingSnapshot`: an epoch per process, a revision, every session, and the memory in use) and `liveStacking:updated` (a session), `liveStacking:closed`, and `liveStacking:products` carry no pixels and no paths; a client drops an event or snapshot of an older revision.
- **Tool.** Images → Live Stacking of a camera tab (see Workspace Responsibility Matrix) mirrors the sessions through `web/src/camera/livestacking.ts` in `SERVER_STORES`: a session by revision, forgotten with an older epoch, and the saved stacks on demand. A color preset is resolved by `presetSources` of `web/src/tools/imagestacking/presets.ts`, shared with Image Stacking: the most recently updated mono stack of each filter of one field, filters recognized by their canonical names.
- Limitations: a session survives no restart and recovers nothing; frames received before a session started are not replayed; a stack is calibrated only with masters chosen before its frames arrive; held candidates of a new field and the masters read count against the memory budget; a color stack of a one-shot-color camera cannot be composed with mono stacks.

## Target Centering Contract

- `TargetCenteringSessions` (`api/src/targetcentering.ts`) brings a fixed J2000 target to the center of one camera, or measures how far it is, inside one `mount.targetCentering` operation: the root holds the camera and a child the mount, both until the end, so nothing comes between an exposure and its correction. The toolkit's `centerTarget` (`core/src/observation/mount/centering.ts`) owns the loop: exposure, solve, measurement, correction, settle. Nothing is measured or corrected in `api` or `web` by other means, and a position is verified only by a solution of a frame taken after the last motion, within the tolerance. An accepted slew or a solve of an earlier position never proves it.
- Modes (`TargetCenteringRequest.mode`): `center` slews to the target and corrects; `recenter` slews only when the position the mount reports is farther from the target than `maximumCorrection`, and otherwise measures first; `verifyOnly` measures, commanding the mount nothing, tracking included, and repeats only a frame without a solution. The request carries the target whole, so a run never depends on a profile, and a later Sequencer can send the same request. A target is J2000 (ICRS) radians; time-dependent targets have no place in it.
- First slew and corrections: the first slew goes through `MountCommands.goToWithin`, with the refusals of the goto route and the single correction of the model in force, and the summary records the model and revision (`model`). Each later correction is the residual measured on the sky, applied from the position the mount reports with the commander directly, so a model is applied once and never stacked. No lock is needed: an activation needs the mount free, and a deactivation or suspension during a session has nothing to reach. Sync is never used, because the commander's Sync neither waits for its echo nor suspends a model.
- Motion checks: the admission (`422`, `409`) asks for a configured solver and, except for `verifyOnly`, `canGoTo`, `canAbort`, a reported site, an unparked mount, tracking or the ability to track (started inside the operation, left on after a success, and stopped after a failure or a stop when the mount is free), and a target above the horizon of the site the mount reports when the session starts. Before each correction the toolkit asks the session, and a correction is refused (`refusal`, moving nothing) for a parked mount, a pier side that changed (when the mount reports one), a distance over `maximumCorrection` (`tooLarge`: centering again is the way past it, since no approval is asked mid-run), or a destination below that horizon. A centering never starts a flip of its own accord beyond what a GoTo of the driver does. The horizon is geometric at 0°, and nothing is known of obstacles, cables, or the limits of the mount.
- Budgets (`TargetCenteringRequest`): `maximumFrames` caps every exposure, failed solves included; `maximumCorrections` caps the motions apart from them, and `noSolutionRetries` the frames repeated at one position, without a motion. Two corrections in a row that did not get closer end the session (provisional). A correction allows 60 s (provisional), and a first slew 5 min, since the commander ends a slew only after seeing it, and a motion of arcseconds might never show on a real driver.
- Frames take the exposure time of the request and the camera settings of the physical camera, snapshotted in it, as one LIGHT FITS frame each with the mount snooped into the header; files are auxiliary, in a temporary directory deleted with the session. Each frame is recorded in the capture frame registry as the latest frame of its camera (origin `targetCentering`, frame id `sessionId:n`) before it is solved with `PlateSolvingService.solve` (origin `targetCentering`), whose provider and settings are resolved once when the session starts and never fall back to another. Once the mount was sent to the target, an assisted search hints the target; before that the header hints it, since the position of a mount is never a hint. `blindRetry` searches the same frame again over the whole sky with the same provider. A frame says what became of it (`outcome`): `solved`, `noSolution`, `invalid`, `timeout`, or `failed`; only a solve past its timeout ends the session by itself.
- Outcome: `completed` with `positionVerified`; a `verifyOnly` that measured the target out of tolerance is `completed` with `positionVerified: false`, a result and not a failure, while a `center` or `recenter` that ends out of tolerance is `failed`. `cancelled` follows `POST /api/target-centering/:sessionId/stop` (which answers 202 at once, since a camera may stay held up to `FRAME_TRANSFER_TIMEOUT`, and again for an ended session) or a stop of either device. An operation that ended with the mount not quiescent leaves the session `blocked`, with its camera free for no other session until the stop dismisses it. `readyForImaging` is what a later automation reads.
- Guiding: a session of the mount's connection that holds a guide output and is guiding is stopped by the operation before it takes the mount (`guiding: 'coordinate'`, the form's default), which needs the operation to exist first, so the root holds the camera while guiding stops, and restored once the operation released the mount (step `restoringGuiding`), whatever the session ended in, unless it is `blocked`. Guiding the owner paused, or that is calibrating, is never taken over, and neither `guiding: 'requireIdle'` nor a `verifyOnly`, which moves nothing, touches any: they answer `409 guidingActive`. `GuidingSessions.hold` keeps guiding from starting until the operation ends. The outcome is `guidingRestoration`: `notNeeded`, `restored`, `failed`, or `externallyChanged` when someone else commanded that session meanwhile, which leaves it as it is. A position verified with guiding not restored stays verified, but `readyForImaging` is false and the operator is warned, so an automation must not go on as if the whole workflow succeeded.
- Sessions live in memory, the latest of each camera. `targetCentering:updated` carries summaries, without any WCS, with a monotonic `revision`; `GET /api/target-centering` is the snapshot and `GET /api/target-centering/:sessionId` the complete record, with the solution of each attempt. The start, `POST /api/connections/:id/cameras/:deviceId/target-centering`, takes a token, so a repeated request answers the same session.
- Images → Target Centering (`web/src/camera/TargetCenteringPanel.tsx`, a camera operation of camera tabs only, with any camera of the connection and the MOUNT of the setup) holds Center, Recenter, and Verify Only as actions of one tool. The target is explicit and always shown: right ascension and declination typed in J2000, the catalog object selected in Sky (`catalogTarget`, shared with Target Offset; its J2000 position at epoch J2000.0, without proper motion, and solar-system bodies, minor bodies, constellations, and the mount are refused with the reason), or a saved framing. Nothing replaces it with where the mount points or with the latest solved center. Recenter is disabled, with the reason, until a saved framing is selected. Center and Recenter ask for a confirmation that names the mount and the target; Verify Only, which commands nothing, asks for none, and starting any of them is the only thing here that commands a device. The exposure time is the form's; the other camera settings are shown and belong to Camera Acquisition (`CameraAcquisitionSection`, the Acquisition section of every camera operation that takes frames), and the solver is shown and belongs to Images → Plate Solver, each with a link that only selects the tool on the same tab. The mirror (`targetCenteringRuntime`, in `SERVER_STORES`) follows sessions by camera, by revision, leaving what an event changed during a refresh, and fetches the complete record when a new solution or an end is reported. While a session runs the panel shows what it applied, whichever tab started it, and a blocked session offers Dismiss. Diagnostics, collapsed, lists each attempt and draws them around the target on a bullseye, north up and east to the left as the sky looks, to the scale of its rings, so nothing is magnified without saying so. On the frame a session measured, the viewer draws the target with `TargetOffsetOverlay` from the solution of that attempt, matched by image id, never from the solution chosen for manual inspection; it needs nothing from Target Offset's own target.
- A caller inside the process admits a session with `admit(connectionId, deviceId, request, invocation)`, which answers a `CommandRefusal` (with `guidingActive` among its codes) or the session and operation ids and `ended`, the final summary once guiding was restored (a blocked session ends only once dismissed). Under an invocation, the camera and the mount must be reserved by it (a mount a guiding run holds counts once it is reserved), and the root, its motion, and the tracking stop after a failure run inside the reservation; the session still suspends and restores guiding itself, so a caller never reimplements that. `interrupt(sessionId)` is the in-process stop.
- Limitations: proper motion and solar-system bodies are not followed; sessions do not survive a restart, and nothing resends a motion after one; a session does not save its frames; only the position is centered, never the orientation.

## Guiding Contract

- `GuidingSessions` (`api/src/guiding.ts`) runs native guiding, without any PHD2 process, for one guide camera and one guide output at a time per camera: one toolkit `InteractiveGuidingSession` (`core/src/observation/guiding/session.interactive.ts`) over a `GuiderClient` of its own, from the first looped exposure until the stop route or the loss of a device. The toolkit owns the states, the tracking, the calibration, the controller, the pulse admission and the settle; the service only forwards commands, publishes the snapshot, the native events and the telemetry, and previews the frames. The guide camera and the guide output come from the request as exact device ids; changing roles or profiles never touches a running session.
- Resources: the session's root holds the guide camera for the whole session, published as a `camera.guiding` operation; while guiding (from Start Guiding until Stop Guiding, the stop, or the loss), its child run holds the physical device behind the guide output (the mount or the camera of its ST4 port) and its logical lifecycle resource, published as `guideOutput.guiding`. Every pulse goes through `GuideOutputCommander.dispatch` under that run (the commander of `GuideOutputCommands`), so ending the run waits for the observed end of every pulse and leaves the device blocked with the `guidePulse` cause when that end cannot be observed. While only looping, manual pulses, nudges and slews of the mount stay possible; the camera stays held. Losing the guide camera or the guide output ends the session as lost (`failure.reason` `disconnected` or `removed`); a reconnected device needs a new session.
- Frames: each guide BLOB is decoded on a worker thread of the session (`api/src/guiding.worker.ts`, bundled as an extra entry point of the executable), which takes the grayscale copy (`luminanceImage` of `api/src/imagedecode.ts`, shared with the image worker), detects its stars with the toolkit detector and the built-in settings of the request and samples their peaks (while the run holds the guide output and the previous frame was measured, only in squares of half side `GUIDING_REGION_RADIUS` (24 px) around the measured star, the lock, and the `GUIDING_REGION_STARS` (16) accepted stars of highest SNR, padded for the detector and merged where they overlap (`api/src/guideregions.ts`), keeping only detections from the tracker's minimum SNR, since the frame quality the guider gates on is the accepted fraction of the detections and the noise peaks of the crops would otherwise sink it, about 30 ms instead of 500 ms on a 1280 × 1024 frame; looping, selecting, a lost frame, and ASTAP use the whole frame), and answers the image, the stars and the FITS/XISF bytes; the tracker reads the stars synchronously. A session whose detector is ASTAP has the worker decode only and runs ASTAP's extraction on the frame's FITS file in a temporary directory of the session, removed at its end, about half a second per frame, sampling the peaks on the grayscale copy; a run in flight is killed by the end of the session. The bytes of the newest frame are opened as a capture document and recorded as the latest frame of the camera (origin `guiding`, never saved); only the newest frame waits while another one is opened, so the preview never holds the guiding back. `guiding:frame` names the frame's `imageId` with its overlay (lock and search positions, search square, measurement, primary star, detected and accepted stars with their HFD), in pixels of the full decoded frame from its top-left; it is sent before the frame becomes the latest of the camera, so a tab has the overlay when it switches to it, and the tab keeps the overlay of the frame before until then, so the markers never blink.
- `POST /api/connections/:id/cameras/:deviceId/guiding` starts a session (`GuidingStartRequest`: the guide output, the exposure in ms, the camera settings sent once, the tracking mode, optional optics, calibration and controller overrides passed to the toolkit unchanged, the guider client settings (`GuidingClientSettings`: dither pattern, search square side in pixels clamped to [16, 128], sticky lock position, whether the RA return of a calibration and a dither are followed by a fast recenter (PHD2's, on by default in the toolkit), and whether a meridian flip of the calibration reverses the DEC output, which no route triggers yet), the devices snooped into the guide frame headers, set once when the session starts, and the star detector with its settings, the built-in defaults when absent); 202 with `sessionId` and `operationId`, and a repeated token answers the same session; 404 for an unknown camera or guide output, 409 when one is disconnected or the camera is held, 422 when the guide output cannot pulse guide or the detector is ASTAP while it is not configured.
- Durable commands answer 202 with a `commandId` once accepted (a repeated token answers the same command) and report their outcome as the `command` of the session record: `POST /api/guiding/:sessionId/start` (recalibrate, settle), `/stop-guiding`, `/pause` (`corrections` keeps exposing, `all` stops the exposures; a pause during calibration is always full and its resume calibrates again), `/resume` (settle), and `/dither` (amount in guide pixels or radians, RA only, guided settle; as in PHD2 the toolkit moves the lock from where it is, reflects an offset that would take it out of the frame, clears the controllers, and, with fast recenter, moves the star straight to the new lock in steps of at most 0.7 of the search half-side before the corrections resume). Immediate commands answer 200 or 204, or the failure of the toolkit: `/target` (a pixel of the current frame, or the best star of the whole frame, even when one is already selected), `/target/clear` and `/calibration/clear` (422 while guiding), and `/exposure` (ms). `POST /api/guiding/:sessionId/stop` ends the session once the guide camera and output were released (204), or answers the failure when the end of a pulse could not be observed. Commands of an ended or unknown session answer 404.
- `GET /api/guiding` lists the latest session of each guide camera; `GET /api/guiding/:sessionId` is its record (`GuidingSessionSummary`: the snapshot, the guide exposure, the solved calibration with its rates in pixels per ms and angles in radians, the progress of a running calibration (`calibrating`, from the diagnostics of each `calibrationStep`: the phase, and per leg the steps against their limit and the travel in pixels against what it needs, RA `minNetRaTravelPx`, the return within `maxClearingOffsetPx`, DEC `minNetDecTravelPx`, and, when the calibration returns along DEC once solved as PHD2's south leg (the toolkit default), the DEC return steps with the distance to the start, with the backlash so far and the unusable frames, cleared once the guider leaves `calibrating`), the RMS of the latest `GUIDING_RMS_WINDOW` (50) measured guide steps in guide pixels, the pixel scale in arcsec per pixel when the optics are known, the progress of the guide exposure the camera counts down (`exposureProgress`, microseconds and percent, from the camera's exposure countdown while it exposes), the Star Profile of the guide star of the latest measured frame (`star`: the toolkit's `measureStarProfile` at the measurement on the grayscale copy, with its horizontal and vertical cuts of ±12 px less the background, `api/src/guidingprofile.ts`; intensities normalized, `adu` converting them), the latest frame and command, the latest event sequence and telemetry cursor, and a revision). `GET /api/guiding/:sessionId/events?after=` and `/telemetry?after=` page the control events (every native event except the per-frame ones, at most 500) and the telemetry samples (one per guide step or lost frame, at most 2000), with `truncated` when the requested position was already forgotten. Events: `guiding:updated` (the record), `guiding:event` (one control event and its sequence), `guiding:telemetry` (one sample and its cursor), and `guiding:frame`. A tab that reconnects reads the record, then the events and telemetry after the positions it has.
- The web store `web/src/camera/guiding.ts` (`guidingRuntime`, in `SERVER_STORES`) mirrors the latest session of each guide camera from `guiding:updated` and the snapshot, ordered by revision, and keeps the control events and telemetry of those sessions only (at most as many as the server), appending each one at its position and filling any gap, or a reconnection, from the pages after the last position it has; a truncated page replaces what it had. `guiding:frame` keeps the newest frame of each session with its overlay; there is no route for the overlay of an earlier frame, so a reload shows it from the next frame. `guidingRequest` builds the start from the profile's guiding form, sending exposures in ms.
- Other services: `runsOn(connectionId)` lists the sessions of a connection holding a guide output, and `hold(connectionId, holder)` makes Start Guiding, resume, and dither of that connection answer `409 busy`, naming the first holder still holding it as the one moving the mount, until every hold is disposed, while looping goes on. Both work by connection, because a guide output on the ST4 port of a camera shares no device with the mount it moves, so the arbiter cannot refuse a slew during its corrections. `suspendForMotion(sessionId)` stops the corrections of a session, keeping its loop and calibration, and resolves once the guide output was released; `restoreAfterMotion` starts guiding again only if that stop is still the latest command of the session: it clears the lock, since a pixel means nothing after a slew, waits for two frames, the first of which may have been exposing during the motion, selects the best star, and recalibrates with the settle of the latest Start Guiding (`RESTORE_SETTLE`, 1.5 px for 10 s within 60 s, when the session never asked for one), since the calibration does not follow the declination. An abort stops waiting and stops guiding. Only Target Centering uses `suspendForMotion` and `restoreAfterMotion`, and they answer results, never responses, so its outcome does not depend on the record of the latest command, which another tab may replace. `claimDither(sessionId, holder)` gives the dither commands of a living session to one claimant until it is disposed: the claim's own `admitDither` is the only dither admitted meanwhile, while the manual route and a second claim answer `409 busy` naming the holder; corrections, pause, resume, and stop are untouched, and a dither already admitted runs to its own settle. `summaryOf(sessionId)` is the record of a session that still lives.
- A caller inside the process admits a session with `admit(connectionId, deviceId, request, invocation)`, which answers a `CommandRefusal` or the session and operation ids and `ended`, the final summary. Under an invocation, the guide camera and the guide output must be reserved by it, and the root, every run, command, and stop of the session acquire inside the reservation through its token scope (`reservedScope`, the toolkit's exception for a root a service keeps of its own), whoever sends the command, the caller or a tab. `admitStartGuiding`, `admitStopGuiding`, `admitPause`, `admitResume`, and `admitDither` answer the command id and `settled`, the outcome of that exact command once it settled, which the single `command` of the record no longer shows once a newer command replaced it; the routes answer through them. `rootOf(sessionId)` is the id of the root holding the guide camera, which a holder draining its reservation (`drainByReservationOwner`) preserves so guiding, its run included, outlives the interruption of the other work; cancelling the reservation (`cancelByReservationOwner`) ends the session.
- Limitations: the controller and tracking settings are fixed when the session starts (only the exposure changes on a running session); the calibration is kept only within a session and the toolkit does not compensate the declination, so a new Start Guiding after a slew should recalibrate; a target pixel is not checked against the frame the tab shows; sessions, events, and telemetry live in memory and do not survive a restart.

## Mount Pointing Model Contract

- A model maps the coordinates a mount was commanded to onto the sky position it reached. The fit, the prediction, and the inverse correction are the toolkit's (`core/src/observation/mount/pointing.ts`); nothing is fitted in `api` or `web` by other means.
- Frame (`api/src/mountpointing.frames.ts`): a sample holds the exact JNOW values the driver received, as opaque coordinates of the driver, and the plate solved ICRS center reduced to the true apparent topocentric place of the middle of the exposure, without refraction (`apparentFromIcrs`). Refraction, the semantics of the driver, and the mechanics of the mount therefore end up in the fit. The reduction is not the toolkit's `equatorialFromJ2000`, which the mount manager applies to a J2000 GoTo and which leaves aberration out, so a model of that frame would carry up to about 20″ that drift over the year. The error is solved minus command, east and north positive.
- Fitting (`api/src/mountpointing.fit.ts`, `mountpointing.worker.ts`, embedded by `app/build.ts`) runs on a worker of its own, one fit at a time, since the leave-one-out refits take seconds at the sample counts of a run. Samples cross as plain numbers, because a toolkit `Time` cannot be cloned. Cross terms are off by default, because at one site `sinAlt` is an exact combination of `sinDec` and `cosHA·cosDec`, which leaves the default features singular from about 65 samples on; pier terms are used only when the training samples hold both sides.
- Withheld samples never enter a fit. They measure it on sky it has not seen, and the offline gate of a revision (`unusable`, `failed`, `passed`) requires a usable model, at least 6 of them, a withheld RMS under 80% of their RMS before correction, and one on each pier side trained. These limits are provisional until simulator and real-mount runs justify them. A measure a fit cannot give is absent, never zero.
- Persistence (`PointingModelStore` in `api/src/mountpointing.store.ts`): the header of each model is a row of `pointing_models` in the operational database, and its samples, revisions, and verifications are rows of `pointing_samples`, `pointing_revisions`, and `pointing_verifications`, in order, deleted with the model. Samples, revisions, and verifications are immutable: a save writes only the rows it adds, and one that would change or remove a row already written is a programming error and is refused whole. Every save is one transaction, so saves of a model never interleave, and a save from an outdated revision is refused with its current state. The server records an acquisition left `running` or `paused` as `interrupted` when it starts (`startDatabase`): nothing resumes by itself. Nothing says a model is active. A coefficient that is not finite is read back as `null`. An export holds the samples, the plan, and how the latest revision was fitted, never coefficients or a verdict, so an import is fitted and judged again here, and only its version is checked.
- Model library (`api/src/mountpointing.ts`, mirror `web/src/mountpointing/mountpointing.ts`, in `SERVER_STORES`): `GET /api/mount-pointing/models` lists the summaries and the fits; `GET …/:modelId` is the complete record, loaded on demand; `POST` imports an exchange document; `PATCH` renames; `DELETE` removes; `GET …/:modelId/export` downloads; `POST …/:modelId/fit` answers 202 and adds a revision when the worker ends. A rename and a fit carry `expectedRevision`, and an outdated one is `409 conflict` with the current summary. There is no draft on the server: a draft is the form of a tab until it asks for a fit, and an exclusion is a choice of that fit, so it never changes a sample or an earlier revision. A model a session holds, or one already fitting, cannot be fitted, and one a session or an activation holds cannot be removed (`409 busy`). Events (`mountPointing:modelUpdated`, `mountPointing:modelRemoved`) carry a sequence per process, and the mirror drops older ones and leaves what an event changed during a refresh.
- Acquisition (`api/src/mountpointing.sessions.ts`): `MountPointingSessions` walks the waypoints of a plan (`planPointingWaypoints`, fixed on the local sky as hour angle and declination, leaving 10° around the poles free), computing the right ascension of each one from the sidereal time of its dispatch. Each waypoint is an uncorrected GoTo through the commander under the session's own lease, with `tolerance: 0` so the commander never skips it, and never a Sync; a settle wait; one temporary LIGHT frame, recorded as the latest frame of the camera (origin `mountPointing`) and deleted once solved; and one solve around the J2000 place of the command, with the solver settings resolved for each stretch of work and no hint typed for an image. The pier side recorded is the one the mount reported. A frame without a solution is exposed again once, without a slew; a waypoint without a solution, or whose solution is more than 5° from the command or differs in scale by more than 5% (provisional) from the session's median, is a rejected sample, kept with its reason and out of every fit. Solvers report no quality, so `uncertainty` is never set. Five waypoints in a row without a sample pause the session. A sample is saved before anything moves again. `POST /api/connections/:id/cameras/:deviceId/mount-pointing` starts a session (a repeated token answers it); `GET /api/mount-pointing/sessions` lists the latest session of each camera, `…/sessions/:sessionId` reads one, and `mountPointing:updated` carries each change.
- Each stretch of work is one `mount.pointingModel` operation holding the camera and the mount. `POST …/pause` and `…/stop` answer 202 at once, since a camera may stay held up to `FRAME_TRANSFER_TIMEOUT`, and cancel it, losing the exposure in progress. `…/resume` is a new operation with a token, after the mount, its site, and its state are checked again. An operation that ended with a mount that is not quiescent leaves the session `blocked`, because a failed stop shows only in text: a resume is refused until the session is stopped. A cancel from outside (an Equipment Control stop) or a failure pauses the session with the reason. A camera has one session that has not ended; a session owns its model until it ends. An interrupted or stopped model is continued by starting a session with its `modelId`, refused for another mount or site, which visits what it misses.
- Admission (`422`): the plate solver configured, a site reported, not parked, `canGoTo`, `canAbort`, tracking or able to track (started inside the operation and stopped at the end unless the request keeps it), not `ALTAZ`, and `UNKNOWN` only with the operator's confirmation, since nothing can prove a mount is equatorial otherwise.
- A run with at least 12 training samples is fitted by itself with Auto when it ends. Limitations: the backend knows no obstacle or cable limit the driver does not report, so the sky is the operator's responsibility; sessions do not survive a restart, and their model then reads `interrupted`.
- Images → Pointing Model Builder (`web/src/camera/PointingModelBuilderPanel.tsx`, a camera operation of camera tabs only) acquires samples and never touches a model. It requires the camera to be the Main Camera of the setup, so a model belongs to one optical train, and takes the MOUNT of the setup. The exposure time is its own form's; the other camera settings are shown and belong to Camera Acquisition, and the solver is shown and belongs to Images → Plate Solver, each with a link that only selects the tool on the same tab. Preview Plan asks `POST /api/mount-pointing/plan`; Start asks for confirmation that the mount is clear to move. The mirror (`mountPointingRuntime`, the same store as the model library) follows sessions by camera, by revision, leaving what an event changed during a refresh.
- Pointing Map (`web/src/mountpointing/`): one polar map of the local sky, zenith at the center, north up and east to the right, shared by Images and Tools (`PointingMap`, pure geometry in `skymap.ts`), with the waypoints marked by status, the withheld ones as diamonds, and arrows magnified to show the shape of the errors, never their size. It is the single `Pointing Map` view of the bottom area of a camera tab, offered once its camera has a session; the diagnostics live inside that view, and Maximize shows the same map in a dialog. The training, leave-one-out, and withheld measures are labelled apart, and a measure that does not exist is a dash.
- Tools → Mount Pointing Model (`web/src/tools/mountpointing/`, `/tools/mount-pointing-model`) manages the models of the MOUNT of the active setup, without any camera tab: it lists its models (an explicit choice lists every mount's), shows the map, the measures, and each sample, and refits, imports, exports, renames, and deletes. A model of another mount can be read but never used. The fit form and the samples left out are a draft of the tab until Fit asks for a revision. Build New Model is its only way to Images: it selects the Pointing Model Builder on the tab of the Main Camera, exactly, by `cameraTabKey`, and is disabled with the reason when there is none, never falling back to another camera. Images offers the way back once a session has a model. Nothing here starts an exposure or moves a mount.
- Verification (`POST /api/mount-pointing/models/:modelId/verify`, from the Pointing Model Builder, since it needs the camera) tests one revision that passed offline on the sky, without activating it: a `mount.pointingVerification` operation visits independent targets from the other pattern of plan, and at each one sends the mount uncorrected and then corrected by that revision alone (`correctedCommand`), solving both, as a session of kind `verification` that cannot be paused. It is the only corrected command outside `MountCommands`. The result, added to the revision without touching its samples or coefficients, passes with at least 3 targets and a corrected RMS under the uncorrected one. A target the revision refuses is skipped with a warning.
- Software correction (`api/src/mountpointing.corrections.ts`): a GoTo of `MountCommands` (the slew route, the goto route and its in-process form `admitGoTo`, `goToNow` of Mount Remote Control, and `goToWithin`, which sends the first slew of a Target Centering session from inside the operation holding the mount) asks `MountCorrections.plan` what to do with its target, once, and synchronously, since `goToNow` is answered at once, so a model in force is held in memory. J2000 and galactic targets are reduced to the frame of the samples, and JNOW, ecliptic, and Alt/Az targets are of date already. The command comes from `correctPointingCoordinate`, with the model, the support of the target, the expected pier side, the convergence, and the clamp all checked, since an unusable model reports convergence too, and goes with `tolerance: 0`, so the commander never skips a small one. The corrected target is checked against the horizon again. A target the model cannot speak for, or within 1° of the meridian with pier terms (provisional), is refused with `422 correctionRefused`, and nothing moves. `flip` is never corrected. Sync, manual motion, guiding, tracking, park, and home are not GoTos. Sessions, the corrections of Target Centering, and dithering use the commander directly, so an active model is out of their way by construction, and no flag can turn it on or off from a request.
- Activation (`POST …/models/:modelId/activate`) is explicit and checks everything with the values of that moment: a revision that passed offline, whose latest verification on the sky passed, the same physical mount and site, the mount free, not `ALTAZ`, and the operator's acknowledgment of what cannot be detected (a native pointing model of the mount or driver, and that a mount that reports no type is equatorial). One revision is in force per physical mount, in memory only, and replaces the previous one at once; refits add revisions and never touch it, and a model in force cannot be deleted. `POST …/mounts/:connectionId/:deviceId/deactivate` is immediate, moves nothing, and works whatever the mount is doing. A restart leaves every mount inactive.
- Suspension: a disconnection, a removal, another site by any path, a Sync, a clock sent to the mount, or an admitted INDI write of `ON_COORD_SET`, `EQUATORIAL_EOD_COORD`, `GEOGRAPHIC_COORD`, or `TIME_UTC` suspends the model at once, with the reason. A suspended model never resumes by itself, and GoTos then go uncorrected, with the state shown, so a routine Sync does not block the mount. `GET /api/mount-pointing/activations` and `mountPointing:activation` carry the state and the latest GoTo, telling the target asked for from the command sent, and whether the mount took another pier side than expected.
- Limitations: Mount Remote Control reports the position of the corrected command, so a client sees the offset; a client talking to the driver directly is not corrected; a native model or a Sync made outside Nebulosa cannot be detected; the host clock defines the sidereal time; a model does not improve tracking between GoTos.

## Meridian Flip Monitor Contract

- `MeridianFlipService` (`api/src/meridianflip.ts`) observes one mount per monitor and commands nothing. It reserves no device, starts no operation, and never pauses an exposure, guiding, or tracking. A monitor exists per physical mount (`resourceKey`). `POST …/meridian-flip/enable` takes `MeridianFlipSettings` and answers the monitor. It is idempotent for the same connection and mount id, and the same physical mount through another connection or id is `409 conflict`. `/disable` is idempotent (204). The registry is process-wide: a tab, a profile, a route, or a reload never enables, disables, or redirects a monitor. A removed mount (`device:removed`) or a closed connection (`connection:close`) removes its monitor, and a restart leaves none enabled.
- Inputs: the telemetry of the toolkit `Mount` (position of date and `hasEquatorialCoordinate`, the site the mount reports and `hasGeographicCoordinate`, tracking and track mode, park, motion flags, mount type, pier side) and the host clock. Never the active observing site of a profile, which the server does not know.
    - The hour angle is LAST − RA in (−π, π], positive west, and the forecast follows the signed hour angle, so it never jumps to the next day after the transit.
    - The monitor subscribes to the dispatcher after its other subscribers. `mount:updated`, `device:updated`, and a running motion operation of the mount (`mount.slew`, `mount.goto`, `mount.flip`, `mount.park`, `mount.unpark`, `mount.home`, `mount.findHome`, `mount.remoteMove`) evaluate it at once, and a 1 s timer evaluates every monitor so sidereal time is observed without new telemetry.
- Settings are wall-clock seconds:
    - early warning (900);
    - window minimum and maximum after the transit (300, 900);
    - deadline warning before the window closes (180);
    - settle time (2).

    `evaluateMeridianFlip` of the toolkit classifies them as hour angles (`seconds · SIDEREAL_DRIFT_RATE`, bounded to ±6 sidereal hours, `MAXIMUM_OFFSET_SECONDS`). The server does not validate them, so the tool keeps them within range. `PATCH …/settings` carries `expectedRevision` of `settingsRevision`, and an outdated one is `409 conflict` with the current monitor. The window is a timing preference, never a physical tracking limit of the mount.

- State (`MeridianFlipMonitor`) has three axes and a reason (`MeridianMonitorReason`):
    - availability: `waitingForMount`, `waitingForCoordinates`, `idle` (parked or not tracking), `repositioning`, `settling`, or `monitoring`;
    - timing phase: `unavailable`, `before`, `approaching`, `windowOpen`, or `pastDeadline`, with the instants of the warning, transit, window, and deadline in Unix ms. Quality is `approximate` for a track mode other than sidereal;
    - mechanical assessment: `unsupportedMountType` (ALTAZ, EQ_FORK), `unknown` (UNKNOWN type, no or `NEITHER` pier side, the pole), `notDue`, `alreadyOnExpectedSide`, `flipCandidate`, or `verificationPending` while repositioning or settling. It compares the side the driver reports with `expectedPierSide` of the position. A candidate means a flip may be needed, never that one is safe or authorized.
- Observation context: a tracking, still position stable for the settle time starts a context. The context is kept while the mount stays within `CONTEXT_TOLERANCE` (0.25°, a detection heuristic). A motion, a jump without motion (a sync or another program's GoTo), or a stop of tracking interrupts it. Settling back within the tolerance resumes it, and settling elsewhere starts a new context and episode (`targetChanged`). A refused command interrupts nothing, since only observed state counts. A disconnection forgets the context, and the reconnected mount starts a new one. The episode also changes when the target passes the lower meridian.
- Transitions (`MeridianFlipTransition`, `meridianFlip:transition`, after the `meridianFlip:updated` of the state they describe; the last `TRANSITION_HISTORY`, 100, per monitor at `GET …/transitions`, memory only):
    - `approaching`, `windowOpened`, `flipCandidate`, `deadlineApproaching`, and `deadlinePassed` (the last three with a candidate pending) are published once per episode;
    - `upperMeridianCrossed` is published only between two consecutive monitored samples of the same context, so a GoTo across the meridian is never a crossing;
    - `pierSideChanged` is an observation, and `externalFlipSuspected` is a change across a motion that came back to the same target in the window. Neither is a verified flip;
    - `monitorUnavailable` and `monitorRecovered`.

    `meridianFlip:updated` is published on a material change (availability, phase, quality, assessment, reason, pier sides, tracking, context, episode, settings) and at least every 30 s, so a tab interpolates countdowns from `sampledAt`. The snapshot (`GET /api/meridian-flip`) and the events carry an epoch per process and a revision per publication. A consumer subscribes, reads the snapshot, and checks `contextId`, `episodeId`, and the current state before acting, since nothing is replayed.

- `POST …/assess-exposure` (`MeridianExposureRequest`, seconds) evaluates the monitor and answers `MeridianExposureAssessment`; it starts and changes nothing:
    - `allowed` when the exposure ends before the window closes (with `latestSafeStartAt`) or no flip is pending;
    - `defer` when it would end after the window closes and the window has not opened;
    - `blocked` with a candidate pending in or past the window;
    - `unknown` without a monitored target, without evidence, or for another `expectedContextId`.

    The answer is valid for its context at that instant only.

- Limitations: no flip is commanded (Equipment Control keeps its own flip), and no physical clearance limit exists. A static right ascension only approximates a non-sidereal target. The monitor cannot know the intended catalog object of an external GoTo.

## Notifications Contract

- Notifications are facts about operations, never their state: `NotificationService` (`api/src/notifications.ts`) receives every event of the connections, operations, and sessions as a subscriber of the `ServerEventDispatcher`, after the broadcast (see Routes and Events), and keeps only what `NotificationClassifier` (`api/src/notifications.rules.ts`) asks for. Its own `notification:created` events are broadcast directly, never classified again. Classifying, storing, and publishing never throw into or wait inside the publishing service.
- Classified transitions: lost connections (`connection:close` with cause `lost`, warning); completed or failed captures, Auto Focus runs, Flat Wizard runs, and Dark Library Wizard runs (success or error); failed masters of the Calibration Library, and completed ones a wizard asked for; TPPA ready to refine (info) or failed; each new Flat Wizard or Dark Library Wizard manual step, by `stepId` (warning), and their cleanup warnings; guiding calibrations completed and sessions ended by a failure; failed DARV measurements, by generation; Mount Pointing Model sessions left `blocked` (error), paused by a failure (warning, once per operation), and acquisition runs completed (info); Target Centering sessions left `blocked` (error), failed, and completed with the position verified (success, or a warning when guiding was not restored); failures of the long-running device commands (go to, slew, flip, park, unpark, home, home search, focuser, wheel, and rotator moves, rotator home, cover and dome commands); Meridian Flip Monitor `flipCandidate`, `deadlineApproaching`, and `deadlinePassed` (warning, once per monitor, episode, and kind) and `monitorUnavailable` near the meridian (warning, per transition), never a verified flip. Quiet: progress, telemetry, frames, device events, successful operations, session operations (reported by their sessions), manual moves, pulses, property writes, every `aborted` failure, and the `removed` or `disconnected` failures of a connection closed on request, until it opens again. Sessions read as the fixed text of their failure reason, since errors can hold server paths; an output file that already exists is the one cause described (`failureText`), by its file name and with the way past it. Target Centering quotes its session error.
- Each record (`NotificationRecord`: id, sequence, creation instant, level, stable code, title, message, source with the connection endpoint, optional target) is created once per occurrence key, also across restarts. Text comes from fixed templates, device names, and fixed descriptions of the failure reasons, cut to 80 characters for the title and 240 for the message; driver errors, paths, and payloads are never copied. Targets are typed (`tool`, the Calibration Library with a group or the Meridian Flip Monitor, `cameraTool` with the camera tab tool, or `equipment`), never URLs or commands.
- History: at most 500 records for 30 days in `notifications` of the operational database, each written in its own transaction, which also deletes what the history no longer retains, and published only once committed; the occurrence key is unique, so an occurrence is never recorded twice, and a deleted one may be recorded again under a new sequence. A record that cannot be written is reported on the console and not published. `GET /api/notifications` answers the whole retained history, newest first; there is no write route.
- Web: `notificationsState` (`web/src/notifications/notifications.ts`, in `SERVER_STORES`) mirrors the events and refetches the history on every socket open, keeping the events received during the request. Only notifications arriving live after the first fetch become toasts (`NotificationToasts`, mounted once by the shell; at most 3, success for 2 s, warning for 4 s, error for 5 s; info never). The same host shows a toast of the tab itself when its event socket stays closed for 5 s after it was open (`onClose` of `web/src/events.ts`, `NotificationsState.server`), then a short reconnected toast when it reopens; the server cannot report its own loss, so this toast never enters the history. Read and dismissed states belong to the active profile, which the server keeps (`profile_notification_state`, see Persistence and State).
- Scope: the bell counts and the panel lists the notifications of the active profile's saved connection, matched by endpoint so they still count after it closed, plus application-wide ones; the panel can show every connection. Dismiss all hides, and marks read, the notifications the panel lists under its current filter and scope. Choosing a notification marks it read and opens its target only when it is open now (`web/src/notifications/target.ts`: the camera tab of that camera, or the device of the watched connection); it never switches profile or connection, commands a device, or touches a session.
- Limitations: no browser, sound, or remote delivery; read states follow the profile, so a tab of another profile reads them apart; a camera tab shows only the latest session of its camera, so an older session is not reopened.

## Capture–Sequencer Compatibility Report

- There is no sequencer engine to integrate. It will be an `api` service that orchestrates the tools as they are: it decides when and why a tool runs, and calls the same application services the manual screens call (Camera Acquisition, Auto Focus, Guiding, Target Centering, the device commands), never `CameraCapturer` or a commander directly. How a tool operates its equipment (capabilities, device commands, resources, cleanup, progress, outcome) stays in the service that owns it; a direct `CameraCapturer` or commander call belongs only inside such a service, for the auxiliary exposures and moves of its own procedure.
- Each tool stays usable on its own and never knows the sequencer. A caller composes tools under one reservation of the `ResourceArbiter` (`reserve`, `reservedScope`, `tokenScope`, `drainByReservationOwner`, `cancelByReservationOwner` of `nebulosa`), which is the only arbitration: no second lock, queue, or busy flag.
- A command's outcome is what its service returns for the exact request it admitted, never a `202`, an event, a notification, or the latest session of a camera, which a newer session replaces.
- Nothing survives the process: a crash, stop, or reload ends every run, and nothing is checkpointed, replayed, or resumed (see Operation Lifecycle Rules). Saved frames stay on disk, and a person starts a new run. No table is added for plans or runs before the sequencer itself.
- Camera Acquisition (`api/src/captures.ts`): a start carries a token (`CaptureStartRequest`, `nanoid` of `startCapture`), so a repeated request answers the session it started and never exposes again. `admit(connectionId, deviceId, request, { invocation, beforeFrame })` is the start of a caller inside the process: it answers a `CommandRefusal` or the session and operation ids, `started` (the toolkit's first dispatch), and `ended`, the final session once the capture released the camera (`completed` or `stopped`, where with Preview + Save `saved` equals `captured` since a failed write fails the capture; `aborted`; `failed` with reason and error). The service remembers the last 256 captures by operation id (a repeated token of a forgotten one answers `404`), so `ended` and a repeated token outlive the latest session of the camera, which the snapshot and the events keep showing. `beforeFrame` is the toolkit's asynchronous gate, awaited after the frame sink's (Live Stacking) and the capture's own dither before each next frame and never before the exposure in flight; its failure ends the capture. A request that dithers (`CaptureRequest.dither`) keeps its own cadence inside the capture, so an orchestrator that dithers between capture blocks itself sends none; the dithering service (`CaptureDithering` of `api/src/dithering.ts`) is the boundary it would call for that. Stop and abort name the session: the routes take `{ sessionId }` (`CaptureControlRequest`) and answer 404 when the camera runs another session, and `stopSession` and `abortSession` are their in-process form, so a late command never reaches the session that replaced its own.

Readiness of the services a sequencer would call:

| Service                                                            | State  | Entry point or gap                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------ | ------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `OperationRegistry`, `api/src/commands.ts`                         | ready  | `ToolInvocation`, `admits`, `reserves`, `scope`, `admit` (`finished`), `admitCommand` (see Operation Lifecycle Rules).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| Camera Acquisition (`CameraCaptures`)                              | ready  | `admit` (see above); the manual routes answer through it.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| Auto Focus                                                         | ready  | `AutoFocusSessions.admit` and `interrupt` (see Auto Focus Contract).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  |
| Guiding                                                            | ready  | `GuidingSessions.admit`, the `admit*` durable commands, and `rootOf` (see Guiding Contract). Under a reservation only the start, Start Guiding, Stop Guiding, the stop, and the drain are tested; pause, resume, and dither go through the same scope but have no test of their own, since they need a calibrated run on the simulator.                                                                               |
| Target Centering                                                   | ready  | `TargetCenteringSessions.admit` and `interrupt` (see Target Centering Contract).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| Mount, Filter Wheel                                                | ready  | `MountCommands.admitGoTo` (the refusals and the single correction of the goto route) and `WheelCommands.admitMove` admit under an invocation; the other device commands stay manual until a caller needs them, `MountCommands.flip` included (no `admitFlip`). The read-only Meridian Flip Monitor already offers what a caller deciding a flip reads: its snapshot, `meridianFlip:transition`, and `assessExposure`. |
| Server events (`ServerEventDispatcher`, `api/src/events.ts`)       | ready  | `publish` of `api/src/index.ts` goes through the dispatcher (see Routes and Events); no event of a sequencer exists yet.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| TPPA, DARV, Mount Pointing Model                                   | future | No in-process `admit` under an invocation: only their routes start them, and TPPA and DARV need a person adjusting the mount.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| Flat Wizard, Dark Library Wizard                                   | future | Their manual steps (`continue` of a pending step, a pause) wait for a person; unattended automation cannot assume they happen.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        |
| Rotator, Dome, Cover, Flat Panel, cooling, Plate Solving, stacking | future | Not needed until the sequencer itself; read-only and analysis services need no operation contract.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |

- `api/src/composition.test.ts` proves the composition with direct calls of the services, no engine, plan, or step: under one reservation of the camera, focuser, and filter wheel, a saved capture, a filter change, and another saved capture, while the manual commands of the same devices stay refused and nothing is left once the reservation is cancelled, and the same with an Auto Focus after the first capture (only with `RUN_ALL_TESTS`); a device the reservation does not hold is refused to its tools; a disconnection fails the reserved work and refuses the next admission; and stopping the operations as a server stop does (`OperationRegistry.stop`) aborts it and resumes nothing. The guiding drain and the Target Centering composition are tested in their own files. The physical exclusivity of a camera and its integrated filter wheel (one `resourceKey`) is not simulated: the simulator's devices are separate hardware, and the arbiter's own tests cover shared keys.

- Acquisition exists once, in `CameraCapturer`: Camera Acquisition, Auto Focus, TPPA, DARV, Flat Wizard, Dark Library Wizard, the Mount Pointing Model, and Target Centering expose through the one capturer of `CameraCaptures` (`captures.capturer`), never an exposure loop of their own; only guiding exposes through the toolkit's `GuiderClient`.
- Every acquisition snoops the managed devices of the equipment setup (mount, focuser, filter wheel, rotator; `captureSnoop` in `web/src/camera/capture.ts`, chosen per camera by `CameraOperationContext.snoop`) into the frame headers. Requests name them by device id on the camera's connection (`CaptureSnoop`), and `api` resolves the ids to the driver names (`snoopedDevices`); snooped devices are read, never acquired.
- The Camera Simulator of the `SIMULATOR` connection (`api/src/connection.ts`) reads the devices a capture snoops through the shared managers, and starts on the `SKY` catalog source: the J2000 stars of the sky catalog around the mount's pointing (`catalogStars` in `api/src/sky/catalog.ts`, `skyStarSource`), through a 50 mm f/2 lens so the magnitude-8 catalog fills its 7.6° × 6.1° field with enough stars to plate solve. The Guide Camera Simulator renders the same sky through a 40 mm lens with a 2 s noise reference exposure, and the Camera Simulator with 3 s (`noiseExposureTime`, 60 s in the toolkit), since star fluxes scale with the exposure over that reference and exposures of seconds would otherwise show no star above the noise. Proper motion is not applied.
- A run resolves its connection and device ids when it starts and keeps them. Changing the active profile in a tab never redirects it.

## Persistence and State

| State                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | Owner                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Profiles, presets, panel and view preferences                                                                                                                                                                                | `api`, database (`profiles`, `profile_equipment`, `profile_preferences`, `profile_camera_settings`, `profile_mount_bindings`, `framing_references`); the fields `schema.ts` marks as view state in browser storage (`web/src/profile/view.ts`)                                          |
| Saved connections and the last highlighted one                                                                                                                                                                               | `api`, database (`saved_connections`); the highlighted one in tab memory                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| Observing sites and the site each profile observes from                                                                                                                                                                      | `api`, database (`observing_sites`, `profiles.active_site_id`); the site a tool chooses for itself in tab memory                                                                                                                                                                        |
| Active profile                                                                                                                                                                                                               | browser storage (`web/src/profile/view.ts`): the tab across its reloads, and the profile used last in the browser for a new tab                                                                                                                                                         |
| Route                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | URL path                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| Document view, zoom/pan, camera tab presentation, astrometric measurements, markers, and target                                                                                                                              | tab memory; the tool of each camera tab and the camera tab selected last in browser storage (`web/src/profile/view.ts`)                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Panel sections expanded                                                                                                                                                                                                      | browser storage (`web/src/profile/view.ts`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
| `sunState`, `moonState`, `skyImageState`, `closeApproachesState`, `artificialSatellitesState`, `skySatelliteState`                                                                                                           | tab memory                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| Target Centering target being typed                                                                                                                                                                                          | browser storage (`web/src/profile/view.ts`), the tab across its reloads                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Image pixels                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | `api` image store, memory only                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Star detection, streak detection, plate solving, and annotation jobs and results                                                                                                                                             | `api` analysis services, memory only                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| Image processing jobs and unclaimed outputs                                                                                                                                                                                  | `api/src/imageprocessing.ts`, memory only                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| Captured frame files                                                                                                                                                                                                         | `api`, `captures/` under the data directory by default (`DEFAULT_CAPTURES_DIR`)                                                                                                                                                                                                         |
| Saved capture frames (metadata, no pixels)                                                                                                                                                                                   | `api`, database (`capture_frames`), one row per saved frame, written when its file is                                                                                                                                                                                                   |
| Capture frame leases                                                                                                                                                                                                         | `api`, memory, per client id                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| INDI property subscriptions                                                                                                                                                                                                  | `api`, memory, per socket                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               |
| External tool settings and the nova session                                                                                                                                                                                  | `api`, database (`external_tool_settings`); the nova session in server memory only                                                                                                                                                                                                      |
| Focuser backlash compensation                                                                                                                                                                                                | `api`, database (`focuser_backlash`)                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                    |
| Mount pointing models: samples, revisions, verifications (no pixels, no active state)                                                                                                                                        | `api`, database (`pointing_models`, `pointing_samples`, `pointing_revisions`, `pointing_verifications`); activation in server memory                                                                                                                                                    |
| Calibration Library: frames and masters (jobs stay in memory)                                                                                                                                                                | `api`, database (`calibration_sources`, `calibration_masters`, `calibration_master_sources`, `calibration_master_dependencies`); `master.fit` under `calibration-library/products/` of the data directory                                                                               |
